\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}{a_{ij}\frac{u_{j}(t+\frac{z_{n}}{c},x)}{e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e+z_{n})}\psi_{j}^{c}(x)}\frac{\psi_{j}^{c}(x)}{\psi_{i}^{c}(x)}}+h_{i}(x,\bm{u}(t+\frac{z_{n}}{c},x))u_{i}^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}{a_{ij}\frac{\psi_{j}^{c}}{\psi_{i}^{c}}}u_{j}^{n}+h_{i}(x,\bm{u}(t+\frac{z_{n}}{c},x))u_{i}^{n},

using an induction argument,
\{u_{i}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in

C^{1,2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N})
to a function
u_{i}^{*}\geq 0
for each
i=2,3,\cdots,m
,
and

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial u_{i}^{*}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{i}\Delta u_{i}^{*}+\left(q_{i}+2d_{i}\left(\frac{\nabla\psi_{i}^{c}}{\psi_{i}^{c}}-\lambda_{c}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{*}-\sigma_{i}u_{i}^{*},\\
u_{i}^{*}(t,x)=u_{i}^{*}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p\right),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \forall\;p\in\mathcal{L}.\end{cases}

Since
\sigma_{i}>0
, the maximum principle then yields that
u_{i}^{*}\equiv 0

in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
. Therefore

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(y_{n},z_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n}}\phi_{i}^{c}(y_{n})}=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(y_{n},z_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n}}\psi_{i}^{c}(y_{n})}\cdot\frac{\psi_{i}^{c}(y_{n})}{\phi_{i}^{c}(y_{n})}=u_{i}^{*}\left(\frac{y^{*}\cdot e}{c},y^{*}\right)\cdot\frac{\psi_{i}^{c}(y^{*})}{\phi_{i}^{c}(y^{*})}=0.

Denote

\varepsilon_{i}^{n}:=\frac{U_{i}(y_{n},z_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n}}\phi_{i}^{c}(y_{n})}\to 0\ \ \text{as}\ n\to\infty,\ \ i\in I.

(3.8)

Let

\bm{w}(t,x)=W(x,ct-x\cdot e):=N_{r}\theta_{c}e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x).

Then
W(x,s)
is periodic in
x
and nondecreasing in
s
, and
it follows from Lemma
3.2
that
\bm{w}>\bm{0}
is a supersolution
of (
1.4
) in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
Hence

\overline{\bm{u}}(t,x)=\overline{U}(x,ct-x\cdot e):=\min\{\bm{w}(t,x),\bm{1}\}

is an irregular supersolution of (
1.4
) in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
Furthermore, there exists
\bar{\sigma}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\overline{U}(x,s)=\bm{1}
for all
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[\bar{\sigma},\infty)
.
Since
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\overline{U}(x,s)=\bm{0}
and

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to+\infty}U(x,s)=\bm{1}
uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
there exists
(x^{\prime},s^{\prime})
with
s^{\prime}<\bar{\sigma}
and
z^{\prime}\geq 0
such that

\overline{U}(x^{\prime},s^{\prime})\leq U(x^{\prime},s^{\prime}+z^{\prime})<\bm{1}.

(3.9)

Assume without loss of generality that
z^{\prime}=0
.
By Lemma
2.1
and in view of (
3.8
),

U(x,z_{n})\leq N_{r}U(y_{n},z_{n})\leq N_{r}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{i}^{n}\theta_{c}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n}}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Let
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
be such that
z_{n^{\prime}}<s^{\prime}
, and

N_{r}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{i}^{n^{\prime}}\theta_{c}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n^{\prime}}}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)\ll N_{r}\theta_{c}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n^{\prime}}}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}\bm{1},\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Then
U(x,z_{n^{\prime}})\ll\overline{U}(x,z_{n^{\prime}})
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
By Lemma
2.4
, we have
U(x,s)\ll\overline{U}(x,s)
for any

(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[z_{n^{\prime}},\infty)
, which contradicts (
3.9
).
The proof is complete.
∎

The main result of this subsection is stated as follows.

Theorem 3.4
.

Assume (H1)-(H7). Let
U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
be a pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
)
with
c>c_{+}^{0}
. Then there exists
\rho>0
such that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{U(x,s)}{\rho e^{\lambda_{c}s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}=\bm{1}\quad\text{uniformly in}\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
3.3
, we have

0<\rho_{*}:=\mathop{\liminf}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}\right\}\leq\mathop{\limsup}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}\right\}=:\rho^{*}<+\infty.

Next we divide the proof into three steps.

Step 1
. We prove that

\rho_{*}=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}\right\}.

(3.10)

If this is not true, then there exist
\epsilon>0
and a sequence
\{s_{n}\}
such that

s_{n}\to-\infty\ (n\to\infty),\quad\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}s_{n}}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}\right\}\geq\rho_{*}(1+2\epsilon).

(3.11)

Let
\delta_{1}=\rho_{*}(1+\frac{3}{2}\epsilon)
and

\delta_{2}=\delta_{1}\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{\theta_{c}K_{c}}\right\}
,
where
K_{c}
is given by Lemma
2.2
. Define

\underline{\bm{u}}(t,x)=(\underline{u}_{1}(t,x),\underline{u}_{2}(t,x),\cdots,\underline{u}_{m}(t,x))

as

\displaystyle\underline{u}_{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{U}_{1}(x,ct-x\cdot e)=\delta_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e)}\left(\phi_{1}^{c}(x)-n_{0}e^{\epsilon(ct-x\cdot e)}\phi_{1}^{\epsilon}(x)\right),

\displaystyle\underline{u}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{U}_{i}(x,ct-x\cdot e)=\delta_{2}e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e)}\left(\phi_{i}^{c}(x)-\frac{n_{0}\delta_{1}}{\delta_{2}}e^{\epsilon(ct-x\cdot e)}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}(x)\right),\ \ i=2,3,\cdots,m,

where
(t,x)\in\Omega_{s_{0}}^{-}
, and
s_{0}
and
n_{0}>0
are given by Lemma
3.1
.
Since
U_{i}(x,s_{n})\geq\frac{1}{K_{c}}U_{1}(x,s_{n})
for each
i
, it follows from (
3.11
) that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(x,s_{n})}{\underline{U}_{i}(x,s_{n})}>1,\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \ i\in I.

(3.12)

On the other hand, it follows from the definition of
\rho_{*}
that
there exists
\{(x_{n},z_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

z_{n}\to-\infty\ (n\to\infty),\quad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{1}(x_{n},z_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n}}\phi_{1}^{c}(x_{n})}=\rho_{*}.

Therefore there exists
n^{*}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that

z_{n^{*}}<s_{0},\quad U_{1}(x_{n^{*}},z_{n^{*}})\leq\rho_{*}\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\right)e^{\lambda_{c}z_{n^{*}}}\phi_{1}^{c}(x_{n^{*}})\leq\underline{U}_{1}(x_{n^{*}},z_{n^{*}}).

(3.13)

Furthermore, it follows from (
3.12
) that there exists

n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that

s_{n^{\prime}}<z_{n^{*}},\quad\underline{U}(x,s_{n^{\prime}})\ll U(x,s_{n^{\prime}}),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

By Lemma
2.5
, we have

\underline{U}(x,s)\ll U(x,s)
for all
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[s_{n^{\prime}},s_{0}]
,
which contradicts (
3.13
). Therefore (
3.10
) holds.

Step 2
. We prove that
\rho_{*}=\rho^{*}
.
Let
\{(x_{n}^{\prime},s_{n}^{\prime})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be the sequence such that
x_{n}^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}

and

s_{n}^{\prime}\to-\infty,\quad x_{n}^{\prime}\to x^{*}\ (n\to\infty),\quad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime},s_{n}^{\prime})}{e^{\lambda_{c}s_{n}^{\prime}}\phi_{1}^{c}(x_{n}^{\prime})}=\rho^{*}.

Let

u_{1}^{n}(t,x)=\frac{u_{1}(t+\frac{s_{n}^{\prime}}{c},x)}{e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime})}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}=\frac{U_{1}(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime})}{e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime})}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)},

then
\{u_{1}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded, and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{1}^{n}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=d_{1}(x)\Delta u_{1}^{n}+\left(q_{1}+2d_{1}\left(\frac{\nabla\phi_{1}^{c}}{\phi_{1}^{c}}-\lambda_{c}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla u_{1}^{n}-h_{1}(x,\bm{0})u_{1}^{n}+\frac{f_{1}(x,\bm{u}(t+\frac{s_{n}^{\prime}}{c},x))}{u_{1}(t+\frac{s_{n}^{\prime}}{c},x)}u_{1}^{n}.

It then follows that
\{u_{1}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
C^{1,2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N})
,
up to a subsequence, to a function
u_{1}^{*}\geq 0
, and

\frac{\partial u_{1}^{*}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{1}(x)\Delta u_{1}^{*}+\left(q_{1}+2d_{1}\left(\frac{\nabla\phi_{1}^{c}}{\phi_{1}^{c}}-\lambda_{c}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla u_{1}^{*},\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Noting that
u_{1}^{*}(\frac{x^{*}\cdot e}{c},x^{*})=\rho^{*}
and
u_{1}^{*}\leq\rho^{*}
by the definition of
\rho^{*}
, the maximum principle then shows that

u_{1}^{*}\equiv\rho^{*}
for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:t\leq\frac{x^{*}\cdot e}{c}\}
,
and furthermore for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
by the uniqueness of solutions.
Then

\rho^{*}\equiv u_{1}^{*}\left(\frac{x\cdot e}{c},x\right)=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}u_{1}^{n}\left(\frac{x\cdot e}{c},x\right)=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{1}(x,s_{n}^{\prime})}{e^{\lambda_{c}s_{n}^{\prime}}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)},\quad\forall\;x\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}.

Since
U_{1}(\cdot,s)
is periodic, it is readily seen that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\left\{\mathop{\inf}\limits_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s_{n}^{\prime})}{e^{\lambda_{c}s_{n}^{\prime}}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}\right\}=\rho^{*}
,
and hence it follows from (
3.10
) that

\rho_{*}=\rho^{*}:=\rho
. Therefore

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{\rho e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}=1\quad\text{uniformly in}\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

(3.14)

Step 3
. We prove that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{U_{i}(x,s)}{\rho e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{i}^{c}(x)}=1\ \ \text{uniformly in}\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\quad\ i=2,3,\cdots,m.

(3.15)

Let

\displaystyle\eta_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=u_{i}(t,x)-\rho e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e)}\phi_{i}^{c}(x)

\displaystyle=U_{i}(x,ct-x\cdot e)-\rho e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e)}\phi_{i}^{c}(x):=\xi_{i}(x,ct-x\cdot e),\quad i\in I.

By (
3.6
), there exists
C_{1}>0
such that
|\xi_{i}|\leq C_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}s}

for all
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}
and each
i
.
Let

\underline{\tau}_{i}:=\liminf_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\xi_{i}(x,s)}{e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{i}^{c}(x)}\right\}\leq\limsup_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\xi_{i}(x,s)}{e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{i}^{c}(x)}\right\}:=\overline{\tau}_{i},\quad\ i=2,3,\cdots,m.

Then one only need to prove that
\underline{\tau}_{i}=\overline{\tau}_{i}=0
.
Let
\{(\hat{x}_{n},\hat{s}_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
\hat{x}_{n}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
be such that

\hat{s}_{n}\to-\infty,\quad\hat{x}_{n}\to\hat{x}\ (n\to\infty),\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\xi_{i}(\hat{x}_{n},\hat{s}_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}\hat{s}_{n}}\phi_{i}^{c}(\hat{x}_{n})}=\underline{\tau}_{i}.

Define

\displaystyle\eta_{i}^{n}(t,x)=\frac{\eta_{i}(t+\frac{\hat{s}_{n}}{c},x)}{e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e+\hat{s}_{n})}\psi_{i}^{c}(x)}=\frac{\xi_{i}(x,s+\hat{s}_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}(s+\hat{s}_{n})}\psi_{i}^{c}(x)},\quad\ i\in I.

By a straightforward calculation,

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial\eta_{2}^{n}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{2}\Delta\eta_{2}^{n}+\left(q_{2}+2d_{2}\left(\frac{\nabla\psi_{2}^{c}}{\psi_{2}^{c}}-\lambda_{c}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla\eta_{2}^{n}-\sigma_{2}\eta_{2}^{n}+a_{21}\frac{\psi_{1}^{c}}{\psi_{2}^{c}}\eta_{1}^{n}\\
\qquad\quad+\frac{u_{2}(t+\frac{\hat{s}_{n}}{c},x)}{e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e+\hat{s}_{n})}\psi_{2}^{c}(x)}\left(h_{2}(x,\bm{u}(t+\frac{\hat{s}_{n}}{c},x))-h_{2}(x,\bm{0})\right)\eta_{2}^{n},\\
\eta_{2}^{n}(t,x)=\eta_{2}^{n}(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p),\ \ \ \forall\;p\in\mathcal{L},\end{cases}

where
\sigma_{2}=c\lambda_{c}-\kappa_{2}(\lambda_{c})>0
.
Note from (
3.14
) that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\eta_{1}^{n}(t,x)=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{\xi_{1}(x,s+\hat{s}_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}(s+\hat{s}_{n})}\psi_{1}^{c}(x)}=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{\xi_{1}(x,s+\hat{s}_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}(s+\hat{s}_{n})}\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}\cdot\frac{\phi_{1}^{c}(x)}{\psi_{1}^{c}(x)}=0

and
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\bm{u}(t+\frac{\hat{s}_{n}}{c},x)=\bm{0}

locally uniformly in
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, and
\frac{u_{2}(t+\frac{\hat{s}_{n}}{c},x)}{e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e+\hat{s}_{n})}\psi_{2}^{c}(x)}

is uniformly bounded in view of (
3.6
).
Therefore
\{\eta_{2}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
C^{1,2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N})
,
up to a subsequence, to a function
\eta_{2}^{*}\geq 0
, which satisfies

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial\eta_{2}^{*}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{2}\Delta\eta_{2}^{*}+\left(q_{2}+2d_{2}\left(\frac{\nabla\psi_{2}^{c}}{\psi_{2}^{c}}-\lambda_{c}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla\eta_{2}^{*}-\sigma_{2}\eta_{2}^{*},\\
\eta_{2}^{*}(t,x)=\eta_{2}^{*}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p\right),\quad\forall\;p\in\mathcal{L}.\end{cases}

Therefore
\eta_{2}^{*}\equiv 0
in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, and hence

0=\lim_{n\to\infty}\eta_{2}^{n}\left(\frac{\hat{x}_{n}\cdot e}{c},\hat{x}_{n}\right)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\xi_{2}(\hat{x}_{n},\hat{s}_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}\hat{s}_{n}}\psi_{2}^{c}(\hat{x}_{n})}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\xi_{2}(\hat{x}_{n},\hat{s}_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{c}\hat{s}_{n}}\phi_{2}^{c}(\hat{x}_{n})}\cdot\frac{\phi_{2}^{c}(\hat{x}_{n})}{\psi_{2}^{c}(\hat{x}_{n})}=\underline{\tau}_{2}\cdot\frac{\phi_{2}^{c}(\hat{x})}{\psi_{2}^{c}(\hat{x})},

which implies that
\underline{\tau}_{2}=0
.
Similarly, one can prove that
\overline{\tau}_{2}=0
, and therefore (
3.15
) holds for
i=2
.
Note that for each
i=3,4,\cdots,m
, there hold

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial\eta_{i}^{n}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{i}\Delta\eta_{i}^{n}+\left(q_{i}+2d_{i}\left(\frac{\nabla\psi_{i}^{c}}{\psi_{i}^{c}}-\lambda_{c}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla\eta_{i}^{n}-\sigma_{i}\eta_{i}^{n}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\frac{\psi_{j}^{c}}{\psi_{i}^{c}}\eta_{j}^{n}\\
\qquad\quad+\frac{u_{i}(t+\frac{\hat{s}_{n}}{c},x)}{e^{\lambda_{c}(ct-x\cdot e+\hat{s}_{n})}\psi_{i}^{c}(x)}\left(h_{i}(x,\bm{u}(t+\frac{\hat{s}_{n}}{c},x))-h_{i}(x,\bm{0})\right)\eta_{i}^{n},\\
\eta_{i}^{n}(t,x)=\eta_{i}^{n}(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p),\ \ \ \forall\;p\in\mathcal{L}.\end{cases}

By using an induction and similar argument as above,
one can prove that (
3.15
) hold for all
i=2,3\cdots,m
.
The proof is complete.
∎

3.2.
The critical case

In this subsection, we consider the critical case, that is,

c=c_{*}:=c_{+}^{0}.

Denote

\lambda_{*}:=\lambda_{+}^{0}.

Noting that
\lambda\mapsto\kappa_{i}(\lambda)=\kappa_{e}(d_{i},q_{i},\zeta^{i},\lambda)

is analytic in
\mathbb{R}
for each
i\in I
, and

\kappa_{j}(\lambda_{*})<\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})
for all
j=2,3,\cdots,m
by (H6).
Therefore there exists
\hat{\epsilon}>0
such that

\kappa_{j}(\lambda)<\kappa_{1}(\lambda),\quad\forall\;\lambda\in(\lambda_{*}-2\hat{\epsilon},\lambda_{*}+2\hat{\epsilon}),\ \ j=2,3,\cdots,m.

For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle L_{i,\lambda}

\displaystyle=d_{i}(x)\Delta+(q_{i}-2d_{i}\lambda e)\cdot\nabla+(d_{i}\lambda^{2}-q_{i}\cdot e\lambda+h_{i}(x,\bm{0})),\quad\ i\in I.

It then follows from Lemma
1.3
that the periodic eigenvalue problem

\begin{cases}\kappa\phi_{1}=L_{1,\lambda}\phi_{1},\\
\kappa\phi_{j}=L_{j,\lambda}\phi_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}a_{jk}\phi_{k},\quad\ j=2,3,\cdots,m,\\
\phi_{i}(x)=\phi_{i}(x+p),\quad\forall\;p\in\mathcal{L},\ \ \ i\in I\end{cases}

(3.16)

admits a positive periodic eigenfunction

\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda}(x)=(\phi_{1,\lambda}(x),\phi_{2,\lambda}(x),\cdots,\phi_{m,\lambda}(x))

associated with the eigenvalue
\kappa=\kappa_{1}(\lambda)
for any
\lambda\in(\lambda_{*}-2\hat{\epsilon},\lambda_{*}+2\hat{\epsilon})
.
Since the function
\lambda\mapsto\kappa_{1}(\lambda)
is analytic,
it follows from the standard elliptic estimates that the eigenfunction
\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda}(x)
associated with
\kappa_{1}(\lambda)
is also analytic with respect to

\lambda\in(\lambda_{*}-2\hat{\epsilon},\lambda_{*}+2\hat{\epsilon})
.
Moreover, it follows from the definition of
c_{*}
that
\kappa_{1}^{\prime}(\lambda_{*})=c_{*}
.

Let
\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda}^{(1)}(\cdot)=(\phi_{1,\lambda}^{(1)}(\cdot),\phi_{2,\lambda}^{(1)}(\cdot),\cdots,\phi_{m,\lambda}^{(1)}(\cdot))
be the first order derivative of
\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda}(\cdot)
with respect to
\lambda
,
which is again periodic, and
L_{i,\lambda}^{(1)}

be the operator whose coefficients are the first order derivatives of
these of
L_{i,\lambda}
with respect to
\lambda
.
That is,

\displaystyle L_{i,\lambda}^{(1)}=-2d_{i}e\cdot\nabla+(2d_{i}\lambda-q_{i}\cdot e),\quad\ i\in I.

Note that
\kappa_{1}(\lambda)\phi_{1,\lambda}=L_{1,\lambda}\phi_{1,\lambda}

and
\kappa_{1}(\lambda)\phi_{j,\lambda}=L_{j,\lambda}\phi_{j,\lambda}+\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}a_{jk}\phi_{k,\lambda}
for
j=2,3,\cdots,m
.
By differentiating these equations with respect to
\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle(L_{1,\lambda}-\kappa_{1}(\lambda))\phi_{1,\lambda}^{(1)}+(L_{1,\lambda}^{(1)}-\kappa_{1}^{\prime}(\lambda))\phi_{1,\lambda}=0,

\displaystyle(L_{j,\lambda}-\kappa_{1}(\lambda))\phi_{j,\lambda}^{(1)}+(L_{j,\lambda}^{(1)}-\kappa_{1}^{\prime}(\lambda))\phi_{j,\lambda}+\sum_{k=1}^{j-1}a_{jk}\phi_{k,\lambda}^{(1)}=0,\ \ \ j=2,3,\cdots,m.

Let

\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)=\left(\phi_{1,*}(x),\phi_{2,*}(x),\cdots,\phi_{m,*}(x)\right)

be the positive periodic eigenfunction of (
3.16
) associated with
\kappa=\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})
.
Let
\epsilon_{*}
be a fixed constant such that

0<\epsilon_{*}\leq\min\left\{\hat{\epsilon},\frac{\lambda_{*}}{2}\right\},

(3.17)

and

\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)=\left(\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}(x),\phi_{2,\epsilon_{*}}(x),\cdots,\phi_{m,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right){{}^{T}}

be the positive periodic eigenfunction of (
3.16
) associated with
\kappa=\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})
.
It follows from the definition of
\lambda_{*}
and the convexity of
\kappa_{1}(\cdot)
that

\sigma_{*}:=c_{*}(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})-\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})<0.

(3.18)

Denote

M_{*}=\max_{i\in I}\left\{\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{i,*}(x)\right\},\quad m_{*}=\min_{i\in I}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{i,*}(x)\right\},\quad\theta_{*}=\frac{M_{*}}{m_{*}},

M_{*}^{(1)}=\max_{i\in I}\left\{\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}|\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)|\right\},\quad M_{\epsilon_{*}}=\max_{i\in I}\left\{\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right\},\quad m_{\epsilon_{*}}=\min_{i\in I}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right\}.

Lemma 3.5
.

Assume (H1)-(H6). Then there exists
s_{*}\in\mathbb{R}
such that for any
0<\delta_{2}\leq\delta_{1}

and
s_{0}=s_{0}(\delta_{1})\leq s_{*}
sufficiently small, there exist
m_{0}=m_{0}(\delta_{1})>0
and
n_{0}=n_{0}(\delta_{1})>0
such that the function

\underline{\bm{u}}(t,x)=(\underline{u}_{1}(t,x),\underline{u}_{2}(t,x),\cdots,\underline{u}_{m}(t,x))

defined by

\displaystyle\underline{u}_{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{U}_{1}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\phi_{1,*}(x)-m_{0}\phi_{1,*}(x)-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}(x)+n_{0}e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right),

\displaystyle\underline{u}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{U}_{i}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)

\displaystyle=\delta_{2}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\phi_{i,*}(x)-\frac{m_{0}\delta_{1}}{\delta_{2}}\phi_{i,*}(x)-\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)+\frac{n_{0}\delta_{1}}{\delta_{2}}e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right),

\displaystyle i

\displaystyle=2,3,\cdots,m

is a subsolution of (
1.4
) for

(t,x)\in\Omega_{s_{0},*}^{-}:=\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq s_{0}\}
,
where
\epsilon_{*}
is given by (
3.17
).
Moreover,

\mathop{\sup}\limits_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,s_{0}]}\underline{U}(x,s)\ll\bm{1},\qquad\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\underline{U}(x,s_{0})\leq\bm{0}.

Proof.

Let
\hat{s}\leq 0
be such that

\frac{\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*}}{2}s+2\ln|s|\leq 0,\quad\forall\;s\leq\hat{s},

and

\hat{s}\leq\frac{2}{\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*}}\ln\frac{|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}}}{6^{2}\gamma_{0}M_{*}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|},

where

\gamma_{0}:=\max_{i,j\in I}\left\{\max_{(x,\bm{u})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[\bm{-\theta},\bm{\theta}]}\left|\frac{\partial h_{i}(x,\bm{u})}{\partial u_{j}}\right|\right\},\quad\ \bm{\theta}=\frac{4}{3}\bm{1}.

Let

s_{*}=\min\left\{-1,-\frac{1}{\lambda_{*}},-\frac{M_{*}^{(1)}}{m_{*}},\hat{s}\right\},

and
s_{0}\leq s_{*}
be such that

\frac{e^{-\lambda_{*}s_{0}}}{3|s_{0}|M_{*}}\geq\delta_{1},\quad n_{0}:=\frac{e^{-\epsilon_{*}s_{0}}m_{*}}{M_{\epsilon_{*}}}\geq\delta_{1},\quad m_{0}:=3|s_{0}|.

Noting that
n_{0}e^{\epsilon_{*}s_{0}}\phi_{k,\epsilon_{*}}\leq\phi_{k,*}\leq|s_{0}|\phi_{k,*}
, and

\left||s|\phi_{k,*}-m_{0}\phi_{k,*}-\phi_{k,*}^{(1)}+n_{0}e^{\epsilon_{*}s}\phi_{k,\epsilon_{*}}\right|\leq 6|s|\phi_{k,*},\quad\forall\;s\leq s_{0},\ \ k\in I,

and

|s|^{2}e^{(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s}\leq e^{\frac{1}{2}(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s},\quad\forall\;s\leq s_{0}.

By a direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{1}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\underline{u}_{1}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{1}(x)\Delta\underline{u}_{1}-q_{1}(x)\cdot\nabla\underline{u}_{1}-f_{1}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}e^{\lambda_{*}s}\left\{\left(\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})-L_{1,\lambda_{*}}\right)\left(|s|\phi_{1,*}-m_{0}\phi_{1,*}-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}\right)+\left(L_{1,\lambda_{*}}^{(1)}-c_{*}\right)\phi_{1,*}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\left.+h_{1}(x,\bm{0})\left(|s|\phi_{1,*}-m_{0}\phi_{1,*}-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}+n_{0}e^{\epsilon_{*}s}\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\left[c_{*}(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})-L_{1,\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}\right]\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}-\underline{u}_{1}h_{1}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}e^{\lambda_{*}s}\left[\left(L_{1,\lambda_{*}}-\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})\right)\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}+\left(L_{1,\lambda_{*}}^{(1)}-\kappa_{1}^{\prime}(\lambda_{*})\right)\phi_{1,*}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+h_{1}(x,\bm{0})\underline{u}_{1}-\underline{u}_{1}h_{1}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})+\sigma_{*}n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle=[h_{1}(x,\bm{0})-h_{1}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})]\underline{u}_{1}+\sigma_{*}n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{0}|\underline{\bm{u}}||\underline{u}_{1}|-|\sigma_{*}|n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{0}\delta_{1}^{2}e^{2\lambda_{*}s}\sum_{k=1}^{m}(6|s|)^{2}\phi_{k,*}\phi_{1,*}-|\sigma_{*}|n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{0}\delta_{1}^{2}e^{2\lambda_{*}s}(6|s|)^{2}M_{*}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|-|\sigma_{*}|n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}m_{\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\left\{6^{2}\gamma_{0}M_{*}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}||s|^{2}e^{(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s}-|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\left\{6^{2}\gamma_{0}M_{*}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|e^{\frac{1}{2}(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s}-|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 0,

and similarly,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\underline{u}_{i}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta\underline{u}_{i}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla\underline{u}_{i}-f_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{2}e^{\lambda_{*}s}\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j,*}^{(1)}+\left[\left(L_{i,\lambda_{*}}-\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})\right)\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}+\left(L_{i,\lambda_{*}}^{(1)}-\kappa_{1}^{\prime}(\lambda_{*})\right)\phi_{i,*}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+h_{i}(x,\bm{0})\underline{u}_{1}-\underline{u}_{i}h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})+\sigma_{*}n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle=[h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}})]\underline{u}_{i}-|\sigma_{*}|n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{0}|\underline{\bm{u}}||\underline{u}_{i}|-|\sigma_{*}|n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq n_{0}\delta_{1}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\left\{6^{2}\gamma_{0}M_{*}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|e^{\frac{1}{2}(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s}-|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 0,\quad i=2,3,\cdots,m.

Moreover, since
|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}
is nondecreasing in
s\in(-\infty,s_{0}]
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathop{\sup}\limits_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,s_{0}]}\underline{U}_{i}(x,s)

\displaystyle<\mathop{\sup}\limits_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,s_{0}]}3\delta_{1}|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{i,*}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\sup}\limits_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}3\delta_{1}|s_{0}|e^{\lambda_{*}s_{0}}\phi_{i,*}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 3\delta_{1}|s_{0}|e^{\lambda_{*}s_{0}}M_{*}

\displaystyle\leq 1,\ \ \ i\in I,

and for each
i
, it follows from the definition of
m_{0}
that

\underline{U}_{i}(x,s_{0})\leq\delta_{1}e^{\lambda_{*}s_{0}}\left(|s_{0}|\phi_{i,*}(x)-m_{0}\phi_{i,*}(x)+|\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)|+\phi_{i,*}(x)\right)\leq 0,\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Therefore
\mathop{\sup}\limits_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,s_{0}]}\underline{U}(x,s)\ll\bm{1}
and
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\underline{U}(x,s_{0})\leq\bm{0}
.
The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Assume (H1)-(H7). Then for any constants
k>0
and
n>0
,
there exists
s^{*}=s^{*}(n)<0
such that for any
s^{0}\leq s^{*}
,
the function
\overline{\bm{u}}(t,x)=\min\{\bm{w}_{c}(t,x),\bm{1}\}
is an
irregular supersolution of (
1.4
) in

\Omega_{s^{0},*}^{-}=\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq s^{0}\}
,
where

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{c}(t,x)=W(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)=ke^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)+n\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}^{(1)}(x)\right).

Moreover,
\bm{w}_{c}(t,x){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}>}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\gg}\bm{0}
for all
(t,x)\in\Omega_{s^{0},*}^{-}
,

W(x,s)
is periodic in
x
and nondecreasing in
s
for any
s\leq s^{0}
,
and there exists
k^{*}=k^{*}(n)>0
such that

\mathop{\inf}\limits_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}W(x,2s^{0})\geq\bm{1}
for any
k\geq k^{*}
.

Proof.

We only prove that
\bm{w}_{c}
is a (regular) supersolution of
(
1.4
) in
\Omega_{s^{0},*}^{-}
. For any
k,n>0
, let

\displaystyle s^{0}\leq s^{*}:=\min\left\{-1,\ n-\frac{1}{\lambda_{*}}-\frac{M_{*}^{(1)}}{m_{*}}\right\}.

Then
\bm{w}_{c}(t,x){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}>}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\gg}\bm{0}
for all
(t,x)\in\Omega_{s^{0},*}^{-}
,

W(x,s)
is periodic in
x
and nondecreasing in
s
.
By a direct calculation and in view of (H7), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bm{w}_{c}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=D(x)\Delta\bm{w}_{c}+q(x)\cdot\nabla\bm{w}_{c}+D_{\bm{u}}\bm{F}(x,\bm{0})\bm{w}_{c}

\displaystyle\geq D(x)\Delta\bm{w}_{c}+q(x)\cdot\nabla\bm{w}_{c}+\bm{F}(x,\bm{w}_{c}),

that is,
\bm{w}_{c}
is a (regular) supersolution of (
1.4
) in
\Omega_{s^{0},*}^{-}
.
Let

k^{*}=\frac{e^{-2\lambda_{*}s^{0}}}{(2|s^{0}|+n)m_{*}-M_{*}^{(1)}}>0,

then
\mathop{\inf}\limits_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}W(x,2s^{0})\geq\bm{1}
for any
k\geq k^{*}
.
The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Assume (H1)-(H7). Let

\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)=(U_{1}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e),\cdots,U_{m}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e))

be the critical pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
), then

\mathop{\limsup}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}<+\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\mathop{\liminf}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}>0.

Proof.

Firstly, similar to Step 1 in the proof of Lemma
3.3
, one can prove that

\mathop{\limsup}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}<+\infty.

(3.19)

Hence there exists
B_{*}>0
such that

U_{1}(x,s)\leq B_{*}|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}
for any
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}
.

Next we prove that for
B_{*}>0
large enough, there hold

U_{i}(x,s)\leq B_{*}|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s},\ \ \ \forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R},\ \ i\in I.

(3.20)

Choose

0<\varepsilon<\min_{i=2,\cdots,m}\left\{\frac{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}(x)\right)}{3\theta_{*}},\ \frac{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}{|h_{i}(x,\bm{0})|}}{2}\right\}.

Since
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{c_{*}t-x\cdot e\to-\infty}\bm{u}(t,x)=\bm{0}
,
there exists
Z>0
such that

|h_{i}(x,\bm{u})-h_{i}(x,\bm{0})|\leq\varepsilon,\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\Omega_{-Z,*}^{-},\quad\forall\;i=2,3,\cdots,m.

Define

w_{i}(t,x)=Ke^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\phi_{i,*}(x)-\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)\right),\quad i=2,3,\cdots,m,

where
(t,x)\in\Omega_{\check{s},*}^{-}=\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\check{s}\}
,
with

\check{s}:=\min\left\{-1,-Z,-\frac{2M_{*}^{(1)}}{m_{*}}\right\},

and
K\geq\frac{2B_{*}}{m_{*}}
is such that

Ke^{\lambda_{*}\check{s}}\left(|\check{s}|\phi_{i,*}(x)-\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)\right)\geq U_{i}(x,\check{s}),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \ i=2,3,\cdots,m.

We prove firstly that (
3.20
) holds for
i=2
. Noting that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial w_{2}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{2}\Delta w_{2}-q_{2}\cdot\nabla w_{2}

\displaystyle=h_{2}(x,\bm{0})w_{2}+a_{21}K|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}

\displaystyle=(h_{2}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)w_{2}+a_{21}K|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}-\varepsilon Ke^{\lambda_{*}s}\left(|s|\phi_{2,*}-\phi_{2,*}^{(1)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(h_{2}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)w_{2}+a_{21}K|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}-\varepsilon Ke^{\lambda_{*}s}\left(\frac{3}{2}|s|\phi_{2,*}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(h_{2}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)w_{2}+a_{21}B_{*}|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}

\displaystyle\geq(h_{2}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)w_{2}+a_{21}u_{1},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{2}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{2}\Delta u_{2}-q_{2}\cdot\nabla u_{2}

\displaystyle=a_{21}u_{1}+h_{2}(x,\bm{0})u_{2}+(h_{2}(x,\bm{u})-h_{2}(x,\bm{0}))u_{2}

\displaystyle\leq a_{21}u_{1}+(h_{2}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)u_{2},\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\Omega_{\check{s},*}^{-},

and
h_{2}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon<0
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
The maximum principle then implies that

U_{2}(x,s)\leq Ke^{\lambda_{*}s}\left(|s|\phi_{2,*}(x)-\phi_{2,*}^{(1)}(x)\right)\leq\frac{3}{2}K|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{2,*}(x)\leq B_{*}|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}

for some
B_{*}>0
and
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,\check{s}]
.
Due to the boundedness of
U_{2}
in
\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}
, there exists

B_{*}
large enough such that
U_{2}(x,s)\leq B_{*}|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}
for any

(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}
.
By using an induction argument, and notice that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial w_{i}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}\Delta w_{i}-q_{i}\cdot\nabla w_{i}

\displaystyle=h_{i}(x,\bm{0})w_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}K|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{j,*}

\displaystyle=(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)w_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}K|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{j,*}-\varepsilon Ke^{\lambda_{*}s}\left(|s|\phi_{i,*}-\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)w_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}B_{*}|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}

\displaystyle\geq(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)w_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}u_{j},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{i}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}\Delta u_{i}-q_{i}\cdot\nabla u_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}u_{j}+h_{i}(x,\bm{0})u_{i}+(h_{i}(x,\bm{u})-h_{i}(x,\bm{0}))u_{i}

\displaystyle\leq(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})+\varepsilon)u_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}u_{j},\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\Omega_{\check{s},*}^{-},

one can prove that (
3.20
) hold for all
i\in I
.

Finally, we prove that

\mathop{\liminf}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}>0.

If this is not true,
then there exists
\{(y_{n},z_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
with
y_{n}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
such that

z_{n}\to-\infty,\quad y_{n}\to y^{*}\quad(n\to\infty),\quad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{1}(y_{n},z_{n})}{|z_{n}|e^{\lambda_{*}z_{n}}\phi_{1,*}(y_{n})}=0.

For each
i\in I
, let

\displaystyle u_{i}^{n}(t,x)=U_{i}^{n}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e):

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{i}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+z_{n})}{e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e+z_{n})}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e+z_{n}|\psi_{i,*}(x)-\psi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{i}(t+\frac{z_{n}}{c_{*}},x)}{e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e+z_{n})}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e+z_{n}|\psi_{i,*}(x)-\psi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)\right)},

where
\psi_{i,*}(x)>0
is the periodic eigenfunction associated with

\kappa_{i}(\lambda_{*})
, and
\psi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)
is the first order derivative of
\psi_{i,*}

with respect to
\lambda
at
\lambda_{*}
. That is,

\displaystyle L_{i,\lambda_{*}}\psi_{i,*}=\kappa_{i}(\lambda_{*})\psi_{i,*},\qquad(L_{i,\lambda_{*}}-\kappa_{i}(\lambda_{*}))\psi_{i,*}^{(1)}+\left(L_{i,\lambda_{*}}^{(1)}-\kappa_{i}^{\prime}(\lambda_{*})\right)\psi_{i,*}=0.

It then follows from (
3.20
) that
\{u_{i}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded.
By a direct calculation,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{i}^{n}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=d_{i}\Delta u_{i}^{n}+\left(q_{i}+2d_{i}\left(\frac{\nabla\left(|s_{n}|\psi_{i,*}-\psi_{i,*}^{(1)}\right)}{|s_{n}|\psi_{i,*}-\psi_{i,*}^{(1)}}-\lambda_{*}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{n}-\sigma_{i,*}u_{i}^{n}-h_{i}(x,\bm{0})u_{i}^{n}

\displaystyle-\frac{(\kappa_{i}^{\prime}(\lambda_{*})-c_{*})\psi_{i,*}}{|s_{n}|\psi_{i,*}-\psi_{i,*}^{(1)}}u_{i}^{n}+\frac{f_{i}(x,\bm{u}(t+\frac{z_{n}}{c_{*}},x))}{u_{i}(t+\frac{z_{n}}{c_{*}},x)}u_{i}^{n},

\displaystyle u_{i}^{n}(t,x)

\displaystyle=u_{i}^{n}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c_{*}},x+p\right),\quad\forall\;p\in\mathcal{L},\ \ i\in I,

where
s_{n}:=c_{*}t-x\cdot e+z_{n}
,
\sigma_{1,*}=0
and
\sigma_{i,*}:=c\lambda_{*}-\kappa_{i}(\lambda_{*})=\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})-\kappa_{i}(\lambda_{*})>0

for
i=2,3,\cdots,m
.
By using an induction argument and similar to Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
3.3
,
up to a subsequence,

\{u_{1}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
C^{1,2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N})
to a function
u_{1}^{*}\equiv 0
, and
\{u_{i}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
C^{1,2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N})
to a function
u_{i}^{*}\geq 0
for each
i=2,3,\cdots,m
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{i}^{*}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=d_{i}\Delta u_{i}^{*}+\left(q_{i}+2d_{i}\left(\frac{\nabla\psi_{i,*}}{\psi_{i,*}}-\lambda_{*}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{*}-\sigma_{i,*}u_{i}^{*},

\displaystyle u_{i}^{*}(t,x)

\displaystyle=u_{i}^{*}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c_{*}},x+p\right),\quad\forall\;p\in\mathcal{L}.

Since
\sigma_{i,*}>0
, the maximum principle then yields that
u_{i}^{*}\equiv 0

in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, and hence

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(y_{n},z_{n})}{|z_{n}|e^{\lambda_{*}z_{n}}\phi_{i,*}(y_{n})}=0,\quad i\in I.

The remaining of the proof is similar to that of Step 3 in the proof of Lemma
3.3
,
we omit it here. The proof is complete.
∎

The main result of this subsection is stated as follows.

Theorem 3.8
.

Assume (H1)-(H7). Let
U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)
be the critical pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
). Then there exists
\rho>0
such that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{U(x,s)}{\rho|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)}=\bm{1}\quad\text{uniformly in}\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
3.7
,

0<\rho_{*}:=\mathop{\liminf}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}\leq\mathop{\limsup}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}=:\rho^{*}<+\infty.

Next we divide the proof into three steps.

Step 1
. We prove that

\rho_{*}=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}.

(3.21)

Assume this is not true, then there exist
\epsilon>0
and a sequence
\{s_{n}\}
such that

s_{n}\to-\infty\ \ (n\to\infty),\quad\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{U_{1}(x,s_{n})}{|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}\right\}\geq\rho_{*}(1+2\epsilon).

(3.22)

Let
\delta_{1}=\rho_{*}(1+\frac{3}{2}\epsilon)
and
\delta_{2}=\delta_{1}\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{\theta_{*}K_{c}}\right\}
,
where
K_{c}
is given by Lemma
2.2
. Define

\displaystyle\underline{u}_{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{U}_{1}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\phi_{1,*}(x)-m_{0}\phi_{1,*}(x)-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}(x)+n_{0}e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right),

\displaystyle\underline{u}_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\underline{U}_{i}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)

\displaystyle=\delta_{2}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\phi_{i,*}(x)-\frac{m_{0}\delta_{1}}{\delta_{2}}\phi_{i,*}(x)-\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)+\frac{n_{0}\delta_{1}}{\delta_{2}}e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right),

\displaystyle i

\displaystyle=2,3,\cdots,m

where
(t,x)\in\Omega_{s_{0},*}^{-}
,
s_{0}
and
m_{0}
and
n_{0}
are given in Lemma
3.5
.
Note that
U_{i}(x,s_{n})\geq\frac{1}{K_{c}}U_{1}(x,s_{n})
for each
i
,
it follows from (
3.22
) that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(x,s_{n})}{\underline{U}_{i}(x,s_{n})}>1,\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \ i\in I.

(3.23)

By the definition of
\rho_{*}
, there exists
\{(x_{n},z_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

z_{n}\to-\infty\ \ \text{as}\ \ n\to\infty,\quad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{1}(x_{n},z_{n})}{|z_{n}|e^{\lambda_{*}z_{n}}\phi_{1,*}(x_{n})}=\rho_{*}.

Hence there exists
n^{*}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that

z_{n^{*}}<s_{0},\quad U_{1}(x_{n^{*}},z_{n^{*}})\leq\rho_{*}\left(1+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\right)|z_{n^{*}}|e^{\lambda_{*}z_{n^{*}}}\phi_{1,*}(x_{n^{*}})\leq\underline{U}_{1}(x_{n^{*}},z_{n^{*}}).

(3.24)

Furthermore, it follows from (
3.23
) that there exists

n^{\prime}
such that

s_{n^{\prime}}<z_{n^{*}},\quad\underline{U}(x,s_{n^{\prime}})\ll U(x,s_{n^{\prime}}),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Lemma
2.5
then implies that

\underline{U}(x,s)\ll U(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[s_{n^{\prime}},s_{0}],

which contradicts (
3.24
), and thus (
3.21
) holds.

Step 2
. We prove that
\rho_{*}=\rho^{*}
.
Let
\{(x_{n}^{\prime},s_{n}^{\prime})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be the sequence such that
x_{n}^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
, and

s_{n}^{\prime}\to-\infty,\ \ x_{n}^{\prime}\to x^{*}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}\ \ (n\to\infty),\quad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{1}(x_{n}^{\prime},s_{n}^{\prime})}{|s_{n}^{\prime}|e^{\lambda_{*}s_{n}^{\prime}}\phi_{1,*}(x_{n}^{\prime})}=\rho^{*}.

Define

\displaystyle u_{1}^{n}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{u_{1}(t+\frac{s_{n}^{\prime}}{c_{*}},x)}{e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime})}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime}|\phi_{1,*}-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime})}{e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime})}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime}|\phi_{1,*}-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}\right)},

where

(t,x)\in\Omega_{n}:=\left\{c_{*}t-x\cdot e<-s_{n}^{\prime}-\frac{M_{*}^{(1)}}{m_{*}}\right\}
.
Noting that
\{u_{1}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly bounded, and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{1}^{n}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=d_{1}(x)\Delta u_{1}^{n}+\left[q_{1}+2d_{1}\left(\frac{\nabla\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime}|\phi_{1,*}-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}\right)}{|c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{n}^{\prime}|\phi_{1,*}-\phi_{1,*}^{(1)}}-\lambda_{*}e\right)\right]\cdot\nabla u^{n}

\displaystyle\quad-h_{1}(x,\bm{0})u_{1}^{n}+\frac{f_{1}\left(x,\bm{u}(t+\frac{s_{n}^{\prime}}{c_{*}},x)\right)}{u_{1}(t+\frac{s_{n}^{\prime}}{c_{*}},x)}u_{1}^{n}.

It then follows that
\{u_{1}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
C^{1,2}_{loc}(\Omega_{n})
,
up to a subsequence, to a function
u_{1}^{*}\geq 0
, which satisfies

\frac{\partial u_{1}^{*}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{1}(x)\Delta u_{1}^{*}+\left(q_{1}+2d_{1}\left(\frac{\nabla\phi_{1,*}}{\phi_{1,*}}-\lambda_{*}e\right)\right)\cdot\nabla u_{1}^{*},\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Notice that
u_{1}^{*}(\frac{x^{*}\cdot e}{c_{*}},x^{*})=\rho^{*}
and
u_{1}^{*}\leq\rho^{*}

from the definition of
\rho^{*}
,
the maximum principle then shows that

u_{1}^{*}\equiv\rho^{*}
for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
By similar arguments to Step 2 in the proof of Theorem
3.4
,
we have
\rho_{*}=\rho^{*}
, and therefore

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{U_{1}(x,s)}{\rho|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{1,*}(x)}=1\ \ \text{uniformly in}\ \ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

(3.25)

Step 3
. We prove that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{U_{i}(x,s)}{\rho|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{i,*}(x)}=1\ \ \text{uniformly in}\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\quad\ i=2,3,\cdots,m.

(3.26)

Let

\displaystyle\eta_{i}(t,x)

\displaystyle=u_{i}(t,x)-\rho e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\phi_{i,*}(x)-\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=U_{i}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)-\rho e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(|c_{*}t-x\cdot e|\phi_{i,*}(x)-\phi_{i,*}^{(1)}(x)\right)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=\xi_{i}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e),\quad i=1,2,\cdots,m,

and define

\underline{\tau}_{i}:=\liminf_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\xi_{i}(x,s)}{\rho|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{i,*}(x)}\right\}\leq\limsup_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\xi_{i}(x,s)}{\rho|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{i,*}(x)}\right\}=:\overline{\tau}_{i},\ \ i=2,3,\cdots,m.

By using an induction argument and similar arguments to those of Step 3 in the proof of Theorem
3.4
, one can infer that
\underline{\tau}_{i}=\overline{\tau}_{i}=0
for each
i
, and hence (
3.26
) holds. The proof is complete.
∎

Corollary 3.9
.

Assume (H1)-(H7).
Let
\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
be a pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
)
with
c\geq c_{+}^{0}
, then for each
i=1,2,\cdots,m
,

0<\underline{\lambda}_{i}:=\liminf_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\partial U_{i}(x,s)/\partial s}{U_{i}(x,s)}\right\}\leq\limsup_{s\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\partial U_{i}(x,s)/\partial s}{U_{i}(x,s)}\right\}:=\bar{\lambda}_{i}<\infty.

Proof.

Noting that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{1}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{1}(x)\Delta u_{1}+q_{1}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{1}+u_{1}h_{1}(x,\bm{u}),

by the standard interior estimates for parabolic equations and Lemma
2.1
,
there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial u_{1}(t,x)}{\partial t}(t,x)\right|+|\Delta u_{1}(t,x)|+|\nabla u_{1}(t,x)|\leq C_{1}\sup_{t-1\leq t_{1}\leq t,\ |x_{1}-x|\leq 1}|u_{1}(t_{1},x_{1})|\leq C_{2}|u_{1}(t,x)|.

(3.27)

Since
\partial U_{1}(x,ct-x\cdot e)/\partial s=\frac{1}{c}\partial u_{1}(t,x)/\partial t
,
it follows from (
3.27
) that

\frac{\partial U_{1}(x,s)}{\partial s}/U_{1}(x,s)
is globally bounded in
\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}
,
and hence
\underline{\lambda}_{1}
and
\bar{\lambda}_{1}
are real numbers.
Next we prove that
\underline{\lambda}_{1}>0
.

Let
\{(x_{n},s_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be the sequence with
x_{n}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
, and

s_{n}\to-\infty\ \ (n\to\infty),\qquad\lim_{n\to\infty}\left\{\frac{\partial U_{1}(x_{n},s_{n})/\partial s}{U_{1}(x_{n},s_{n})}\right\}=\underline{\lambda}_{1}.

Up to extraction of a subsequence,
x_{n}\to x_{\infty}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
as

n\to\infty
. Define

\displaystyle u_{1}^{n}(t,x)=\frac{u_{1}(t+t_{n},x)}{u_{1}(t_{n},x_{n})}=\frac{U_{1}(x,ct-x\cdot e+ct_{n})}{U_{1}(x_{n},s_{n})},

where

t_{n}:=\frac{s_{n}+x_{n}\cdot e}{c},\qquad ct_{n}=s_{n}+x_{n}\cdot e\to-\infty\ \ \ (n\to\infty).

By Lemma
2.1
, the sequence
\{u_{1}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is locally uniformly bounded in

\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{1}^{n}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=d_{1}\Delta u_{1}^{n}+q_{1}\cdot\nabla u_{1}^{n}+h_{1}(x,\bm{u}(t+t_{n},x))u_{1}^{n},

\displaystyle u_{1}^{n}(t,x)

\displaystyle=u_{1}^{n}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p\right),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \forall\;p\in\mathcal{L},

and in particular,
u_{1}^{n}(0,x_{n})=1
. Noting that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\bm{u}(t+t_{n},x)=\bm{0}
locally uniformly in
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
by the standard parabolic estimates, the sequence
\{u_{1}^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges
up to extraction of a subsequence in
C^{1,2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N})
to a function
u_{1}^{\infty}\geq 0
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=d_{1}\Delta u_{1}^{\infty}+q_{1}\cdot\nabla u_{1}^{\infty}+h_{1}(x,\bm{0})u_{1}^{\infty},

\displaystyle u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)

\displaystyle=u_{1}^{\infty}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p\right),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \forall\;p\in\mathcal{L},

and in particular,
u_{1}^{\infty}(0,x_{\infty})=1
.
It then follows from the maximum principle that
u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)>0
for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
Since

\frac{\partial u_{1}^{n}(t,x)/\partial t}{u_{1}^{n}(t,x)}=\frac{\partial u_{1}(t+t_{n},x)/\partial t}{u_{1}(t+t_{n},x)}=c\frac{\partial U_{1}(x,c(t+t_{n})-x\cdot e)/\partial s}{U_{1}(x,c(t+t_{n})-x\cdot e)},

by passing the limits and in view of the definition of
\underline{\lambda}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle w_{1}(t,x):=\frac{\partial u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)/\partial t}{u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)}\geq c\underline{\lambda}_{1}\ \ (c>0)\ \ \text{or}\ \ \leq c\underline{\lambda}_{1}\ \ (c<0),

and in particular,
w_{1}(0,x_{\infty})=c\underline{\lambda}_{1}
.
Noting that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial w_{1}(t,x)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=d_{1}\Delta w_{1}+\left(q_{1}+2d_{1}\frac{\nabla u_{1}^{\infty}}{u_{1}^{\infty}}\right)\cdot\nabla w_{1},

\displaystyle w_{1}(t,x)

\displaystyle=w_{1}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p\right),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \forall\;p\in\mathcal{L},

the maximum principle then implies that
w_{1}(t,x)\equiv c\underline{\lambda}_{1}

in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, that is,

\frac{\partial u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)}{\partial t}\equiv c\underline{\lambda}_{1}u_{1}^{\infty}
.
Hence
\frac{\partial(u_{1}^{\infty}e^{-\underline{\lambda}_{1}ct})}{\partial t}\equiv 0
,
which shows that
u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)=e^{\underline{\lambda}_{1}ct}v(x)
.
On the other hand,
u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)=u_{1}^{\infty}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p\right)
for any

(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
p\in\mathcal{L}
, then

u_{1}^{\infty}(t,x)=e^{\underline{\lambda}_{1}(ct-x\cdot e)}\phi_{1}(x)
, where
\phi_{1}(x)>0
satisfies

\displaystyle c\underline{\lambda}_{1}\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=d_{1}\Delta\phi_{1}+(q_{1}-2\underline{\lambda}_{1}d_{1}e)\cdot\nabla\phi_{1}+(d_{1}\underline{\lambda}_{1}^{2}-\underline{\lambda}_{1}q_{1}\cdot e+h_{1}(x,\bm{0}))\phi_{1},

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\phi_{1}(x+p),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \ p\in\mathcal{L}.

Therefore
c\underline{\lambda}_{1}=\kappa_{1}(\underline{\lambda}_{1})
. Similarly,
one can obtain
c\overline{\lambda}_{1}=\kappa_{1}(\overline{\lambda}_{1})
.
Observing that
\kappa_{1}(0)=\lambda_{0}(d_{1},q_{1},\zeta^{1})>0
,
U_{1}(x,s)>0
and
\lim_{s\to-\infty}U_{1}(x,s)=0
, the quantities

\underline{\lambda}_{1}
and
\overline{\lambda}_{1}
are nonzero with the same sign
and cannot be negative. Consequently,

\underline{\lambda}_{1}=\hat{\lambda}>0
,
where
\hat{\lambda}:=\lambda_{c}
if
c>c_{+}^{0}
and

\hat{\lambda}:=\lambda_{+}^{0}
if
c=c_{+}^{0}
, in terms of (
1.12
).

Noticing that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{2}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{2}(x)\Delta u_{2}+q_{2}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{2}+\left(a_{21}(x)\frac{u_{1}(t,x)}{u_{2}(t,x)}+h_{2}(x,\bm{u})\right)u_{2},

where

a_{21}(x)\frac{u_{1}(t,x)}{u_{2}(t,x)}+h_{2}(x,\bm{u})
is uniformly bounded
in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
in terms of Theorems
3.4
and
3.8

and (H1).
Using a similar argument as above, one can prove that there exists
\phi_{2}(x)>0
such that

\displaystyle c\underline{\lambda}_{2}\phi_{2}

\displaystyle=d_{2}\Delta\phi_{2}+(q_{2}-2\underline{\lambda}_{2}d_{2}e)\cdot\nabla\phi_{2}+\left(d_{2}\underline{\lambda}_{2}^{2}-\underline{\lambda}_{2}q_{2}\cdot e+a_{21}\frac{\phi_{1}^{\lambda}(x)}{\phi_{2}^{\lambda}(x)}+h_{2}(x,\bm{0})\right)\phi_{2},

(3.28)

\displaystyle\phi_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\phi_{2}(x+p),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \ p\in\mathcal{L}.

Since
\phi_{2}^{\lambda}(x)>0
satisfies (
3.28
)
with
\underline{\lambda}_{2}=\hat{\lambda}
, the uniqueness of the principal eigenvalue
then implies that
\underline{\lambda}_{2}>0
.
Since for each
i=3,\cdots,m
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{i}(t,x)}{\partial t}=d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i}+q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i}+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}(x)\frac{u_{j}(t,x)}{u_{i}(t,x)}+h_{i}(x,\bm{u})\right)u_{i},

where

\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}(x)\frac{u_{j}(t,x)}{u_{i}(t,x)}+h_{i}(x,\bm{u})
is uniformly bounded
in
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, a similar argument as above shows that
\underline{\lambda}_{i}>0
for each
i\in I
.
The proof is then complete.
∎

To this end, we give the proof of Theorem
1.11
as follows.

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.

The proof follows from Theorems
3.4
and
3.8
.
The proof is complete.
∎

4.
Uniqueness of pulsating traveling fronts

In this section, we always assume that (H1)-(H8) are satisfied,
and we prove the uniqueness of pulsating traveling fronts.
For each
i\in I
, let

\displaystyle\varrho_{i}

\displaystyle:=\sup\left\{\varrho\geq 0:\ \sum_{k=1}^{m}\left|\frac{\partial f_{i}(x,\bm{u})}{\partial u_{k}}-\frac{\partial f_{i}(x,\bm{1})}{\partial u_{k}}\right|\leq\frac{\alpha_{*}|\mu^{-}|}{2},\ \ \forall\;(x,\bm{u})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[(1-\varrho)\bm{1},(1+\varrho)\bm{1}]\right\},

(4.1)

where

\alpha_{*}:=\frac{\min_{i\in I}\{\min_{x}{\psi_{i}(x)}\}}{\max_{i\in I}\{\max_{x}{\psi_{i}(x)}\}},

and
\mu^{-}<0
and
\bm{\Psi}(x)=(\psi_{1}(x),\psi_{2}(x),\cdots,\psi_{m}(x))
are given in (H8).

Firstly, we establish a comparison principle in the region where the fronts are close
to the stable periodic solution.

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume (H1)-(H8). If
\underline{\bm{u}}(t,x)=\underline{U}(x,ct-x\cdot e)

and
\overline{\bm{u}}(t,x)=\overline{U}(x,ct-x\cdot e)

are sub- and supersolutions of (
1.4
) in
C^{1,2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N})
, respectively,
\underline{U}(x,s)
and
\overline{U}(x,s)
are periodic in
x
,
and there exists
s^{*}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\begin{cases}\underline{U}(x,s),\overline{U}(x,s)\in[(1-\varrho^{*})\bm{1},\bm{1}],\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[s^{*},+\infty),\\
\mathop{\liminf}\limits_{s\to+\infty}\left\{\mathop{\inf}\limits_{x}\left\{\overline{U}(x,s)-\underline{U}(x,s)\right\}\right\}\geq\bm{0},\\
\overline{U}(x,s^{*})\geq\underline{U}(x,s^{*}),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\end{cases}

where
\varrho^{*}=\min\{1,\min_{i\in I}\varrho_{i}\}
.
Then

\overline{U}(x,s)\geq\underline{U}(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[s^{*},+\infty).

Proof.

The proof follows a similar argument to that of
[
5
, Lemma 3.1]
,
we omit the details here.
∎

Theorem 4.2
.

Assume (H1)-(H8).
If
\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
and
\bm{v}(t,x)=V(x,ct-x\cdot e)
are
two pulsating traveling fronts of (
1.4
) with
c\not=0
.
Then there exists
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

U(x,s+z_{0})=V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R},

that is, there exists
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}

(\sigma=z_{0}/c)
such that

\bm{u}(t+\sigma,x)=\bm{v}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Proof.

In view of Theorem
1.11
, there exist
\rho_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{U(x,s)}{\rho_{1}|s|^{\tau}e^{\lambda_{c}s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}=\bm{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{V(x,s)}{\rho_{2}|s|^{\tau}e^{\lambda_{c}s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}=\bm{1}\quad\text{uniformly\ in}\ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},

(4.2)

where
\tau=0
if
c>c_{*}
and
\tau=1
if
c=c_{*}
.
Next we divide the proof into three steps.

Step 1
. We prove that there exists
\bar{z}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

U(x,s+\bar{z})\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}.

Let
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{0}}>\rho_{2}
.
By (
4.2
), there exists
M>0
such that

U(x,s+z_{0})\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-M],

and that

|U(x,s+z_{0})-\bm{1}|+|V(x,s)-\bm{1}|\leq\varrho^{*},\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[M,+\infty),

where
\varrho^{*}>0
is given in Lemma
4.1
.
By the boundedness of
V(x,s)
in
\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-2M,2M]
, and note that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to+\infty}U(x,s)=\bm{1}
uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
there exits
\bar{z}\geq z_{0}
such that

U(x,s+\bar{z})\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-2M,2M],

and hence

U(x,s+\bar{z})\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-\infty,2M].

(4.3)

Lemma
4.1
applied to

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{u}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\bm{v}(t,x)=V(x,ct-x\cdot e),

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{u}}(t,x)

\displaystyle:=\bm{u}\left(t+\frac{\bar{z}}{c},x)\right)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e+\bar{z})

and
s^{*}=M
shows that

U(x,s+\bar{z})\geq V(x,s)
for all
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[M,\infty)
,
which together with (
4.3
) yields that

U(x,s+\bar{z})\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}.

Step 2
. Let

z_{*}=\inf\left\{z\in\mathbb{R}\ |\ U(x,s+z)\geq V(x,s),\ \forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}\right\}.

Observe that
-\infty<z_{*}\leq\bar{z}
, and it follows from (
4.2
) that
\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{*}}\geq\rho_{2}

(otherwise, there exist
i_{0}
and
(\tilde{x},\tilde{s})
such that
U_{i_{0}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{s}+z_{*})<V_{i_{0}}(\tilde{x},\tilde{s})
,
which contradicts the definition of
z_{*}
).
Assume that
\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{*}}>\rho_{2}
. Define

\bm{w}(t,x)=\bm{u}\left(t+\frac{z_{*}}{c},x\right)-\bm{v}(t,x)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e+z_{*})-V(x,ct-x\cdot e),

then
\bm{w}\geq\bm{0}
, and for each
i\in I
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial w_{i}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta w_{i}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla w_{i}

\displaystyle=f_{i}\left(x,\bm{u}\left(t+\frac{z_{*}}{c},x\right)\right)-f_{i}(x,\bm{v}(t,x))

\displaystyle\geq\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{i}}(x,s\bm{u}+(1-s)\bm{v})}ds\right)w_{i}

by (H3). If there exist
i_{0}
and
(\hat{x},\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}
such that

w_{i_{0}}(\hat{t},\hat{x})=0
, where
\hat{t}:=\frac{\hat{s}+\hat{x}\cdot e}{c}
,
then it follows from the maximum principle that

w_{i_{0}}(t,x)\equiv 0
for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:t\leq\hat{t}\}
.
Noting that
w_{i_{0}}(t,x)=w_{i_{0}}\left(t+\frac{p\cdot e}{c},x+p\right)

in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
for all
p\in\mathcal{L}
,
then
w_{i_{0}}(t,x)\equiv 0
for any
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, that is,

U_{i_{0}}(x,s+z_{*})\equiv V_{i_{0}}(x,s)
for any
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}
,
which contradicts
\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{*}}>\rho_{2}
in terms of (
4.2
).
Therefore
\bm{w}(t,x)\gg\bm{0}
for all
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
that is,

U(x,s+z_{*})\gg V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}.

(4.4)

On the other hand, one gets from
\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{*}}>\rho_{2}
that

\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}(z_{*}-l)}>\rho_{2}
for any
l\in\left(0,z_{*}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{c}}\ln{\frac{\rho_{2}}{\rho_{1}}}\right)
.
Now fix
l_{0}\in\left(0,z_{*}-\frac{1}{\lambda_{c}}\ln{\frac{\rho_{2}}{\rho_{1}}}\right)
, and let
\theta\in\left(\frac{\rho_{2}}{\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}(z_{*}-l_{0})}},1\right)
.
Since
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}\frac{|s+z_{*}-l_{0}|}{|s|}=1
, there exists

K_{\theta}>0
such that
\frac{|s+z_{*}-l_{0}|}{|s|}\geq\theta
for any
s\leq-K_{\theta}
.
Let

0<\epsilon<\frac{\theta\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}(z_{*}-l_{0})}-\rho_{2}}{2\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}(z_{*}-l_{0})}+\rho_{2}}.

In view of (
4.2
), there exits
K_{\epsilon}>0
such that

\left|\frac{U(x,s+z_{*}-l_{0})}{\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}(z_{*}-l_{0})}|s+z_{*}-l_{0}|^{\tau}e^{\lambda_{c}s}{\bm{\Phi}}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}-\bm{1}\right|\leq\epsilon\ \quad\text{and}\ \quad\left|\frac{V(x,s)}{\rho_{2}|s|^{\tau}e^{\lambda_{c}s}{\bm{\Phi}}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}-\bm{1}\right|\leq\epsilon

for any
(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-K_{\epsilon}]
.
Therefore
U(x,s+z_{*}-l_{0})\geq V(x,s)
for any

(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-K_{\epsilon}-K_{\theta}]
.
Furthermore, for any
l\in(0,l_{0}]
, we have

U(x,s+z_{*}-l)\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-K_{\epsilon}-K_{\theta}].

Let
M\geq K_{\epsilon}+K_{\theta}
. By (
4.4
),
there exists
0<l_{M}\leq l_{0}
such that for any
0<l\leq l_{M}
,

U(x,s+z_{*}-l)\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-M,M].

Now let
M>0
be large enough such that

|U(x,s+z_{*}-l_{0})-\bm{1}|+|V(x,s)-\bm{1}|\leq\varrho^{*},\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[M,+\infty).

Observe that

U(x,s+z_{*}-l_{M})\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,M].

(4.5)

Lemma
4.1
then applied to

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{u}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\bm{v}(t,x)=V(x,ct-x\cdot e),

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{u}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\bm{u}\left(t+\frac{z_{*}-l_{M}}{c},x\right)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e+z_{*}-l_{M})

and
s^{*}=M
, together with (
4.5
), yields that

U(x,s+z_{*}-l_{M})\geq V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R},

which contradicts the definition of
z_{*}
. Therefore,
\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z_{*}}=\rho_{2}
.

Step 3
. Define

z^{*}=\sup\left\{z\in\mathbb{R}\ |\ U(x,s+z)\leq V(x,s),\ \forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}\right\}.

Similar to Step 2, one can prove that
z^{*}
is bounded,
and that
\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z^{*}}\leq\rho_{2}
. Noting that

-z^{*}=\inf\left\{-z\in\mathbb{R}\ |\ V(x,s-z)\geq U(x,s),\ \forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}\right\}.

By changing the roles of
U
and
V
, and following similar arguments as in Step 2,
we conclude that
\rho_{2}e^{-\lambda_{c}z^{*}}=\rho_{1}
, that is,

\rho_{1}e^{\lambda_{c}z^{*}}=\rho_{2}
. Therefore,
z^{*}=z_{*}:=z_{0}
, and consequently,

U(x,s+z_{0})=V(x,s),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R},

which is equivalent to

\bm{u}(t+\sigma,x)=\bm{v}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

The proof of Theorem
1.12
is then complete.
∎

5.
Stability of pulsating traveling fronts

This section is devoted to the study of asymptotic stability of pulsating traveling fronts for solutions of the Cauchy problem

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial\bm{u}(t,x)}{\partial t}=D(x)\Delta\bm{u}+q(x)\cdot\nabla\bm{u}+\bm{F}(x,\bm{u}),\quad t>0,\ \ x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\\
\bm{u}(0,x)=\bm{u}_{0}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\end{cases}

(5.1)

where
\bm{u}_{0}
is a uniformly continuous function
from
\mathbb{R}^{N}
to
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, and
\bm{0}<\bm{u}_{0}<\bm{1}
.
We shall use

\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})=(u_{1}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0}),u_{2}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0}),\cdots,u_{m}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})){{}^{T}}

to denote the classical solution of (
5.1
) with initial data

\bm{u}(0,\cdot;\bm{u}_{0})=\bm{u}_{0}
.
Observe that
\bm{0}\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{1}
for any

(t,x)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
by the maximum principle.
We first state a comparison principle as follows.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
D=\{(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:t>t_{0},\ ct-x\cdot e<s_{0}\}
, where

t_{0}\geq 0
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Assume that
\underline{\bm{u}}
,

\overline{\bm{u}}\in C_{b}^{1+\theta/2,2+\theta}(D)\cap C_{b}(\overline{D})

are sub-and supersolutions of (
5.1
) in
D
, respectively,
and
\underline{\bm{u}}\leq\bm{1}
and
\overline{\bm{u}}\geq\bm{0}
for all
(t,x)\in\overline{D}
.
If
\underline{\bm{u}}(t,x)\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\overline{\bm{u}}(t,x)
for
all
(t,x)\in\partial D:=\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:t=t_{0},\ ct-x\cdot e<s_{0}\}\cup\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:t>t_{0},\ ct-x\cdot e=s_{0}\}
, then

\underline{\bm{u}}(t,x)\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\overline{\bm{u}}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\overline{D}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
35
, Proposition 4.1]
, we omit the details here.
∎

In this section, the initial data
\bm{0}<\bm{u}_{0}<\bm{1}
is assumed to be close to the pulsating traveling front at
t=0
at both ends, in the sense that

\liminf_{\varsigma\to+\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ -x\cdot e\geq\varsigma}\bm{u}_{0}(x)\right\}\geq(1-\varepsilon_{0})\bm{1}

(5.2)

for some
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\frac{\hat{\delta}}{2\delta_{M}})
,
where
\hat{\delta}\in(0,\delta_{m}]
is some constant, and

\delta_{m}=\min_{k=1,2,\cdots,m}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{1}{\psi_{k}(x)}\right\},\qquad\delta_{M}=\max_{k=1,2,\cdots,m}\left\{\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{1}{\psi_{k}(x)}\right\},

with
\bm{\Psi}=(\psi_{1},\cdots,\psi_{m})
given in (H8).
Moreover, there exists
k>0
such that

\limsup_{\varsigma\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\leq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{\bm{u}_{0}(x)}{k|x\cdot e|^{\tau}e^{-\lambda_{c}(x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}-\bm{1}\right|\right\}=0,

(5.3)

where
\tau=0
if
c>c_{+}^{0}(e)
and
\tau=1
if
c=c_{+}^{0}(e)
.

Using a very similar argument as in
[
36
, Proposition A.4]
, we have the following result.

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume (H1)-(H8), and that there exists
k>0
such that (
5.3
) holds.
Let
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}J}\subset[0,+\infty)
be any compact subset, then there exists
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varsigma\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\leq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})-U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0})}{U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0})}\right|\right\}=0\quad\text{uniformly in}\ \ t\in{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}I}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}J}.

Proof.

In view of Theorem
1.11
, there exists
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varsigma\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\leq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{U(x,-x\cdot e+s_{0})}{k|x\cdot e|^{\tau}e^{-\lambda_{c}(x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}-\bm{1}\right|\right\}=0,

where
s_{0}
is uniquely determined by
k
. The remaining of the proof is similar to that of

[
36
, Proposition A.4]
, we omit the details here.
∎

In the following, we study the global stability properties of
pulsating traveling fronts, in the case
c>c_{+}^{0}(e)
and
c=c_{+}^{0}(e)
, respectively.

5.1.
The super-critical case
c>c_{+}^{0}(e)

In this subsection, we consider the super-critical case
c>c_{+}^{0}(e)
.
Let
0<\lambda_{c}<\lambda_{+}^{0}
be such that
\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{c})=c\lambda_{c}
, and

0<\epsilon<\min\left\{\frac{\lambda_{+}^{0}-\lambda_{c}}{2},\frac{\lambda_{c}}{2}\right\}
.
It is easy to see that

\sigma_{\epsilon}:=\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)-c(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)<0,

and there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that
|\sigma_{\epsilon}|\leq|\mu^{-}|

for any
0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}
, where

\mu^{-}<0
is the principal eigenvalue associated with positive periodic eigenfunction

\bm{\Psi}=(\psi_{1},\cdots,\psi_{m})
given in (H8).

Let

0<\epsilon<\min\left\{\frac{\lambda_{+}^{0}-\lambda_{c}}{2},\frac{\lambda_{c}}{2},\epsilon_{0}\right\},\qquad\beta=\frac{|\sigma_{\epsilon}|}{2},

and
\chi(s)
be a smooth function such that

\begin{cases}\chi(s)=0,\quad\forall s\geq\bar{s},\\
\chi(s)=1,\quad\forall s\leq\underline{s},\\
|\chi^{\prime}|+|\chi^{\prime\prime}|\leq 1,\\
\chi^{\prime}\leq 0,\end{cases}

(5.4)

where
\underline{s}<\bar{s}
are certain constants. Define

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}(x,s)

\displaystyle=\chi(s)e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)+(1-\chi(s))\bm{\Psi}(x),

where
\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}=\left(\phi_{1}^{\epsilon},\phi_{2}^{\epsilon},\cdots,\phi_{m}^{\epsilon}\right){{}^{\top}}

is the positive periodic eigenfunction of (
1.13
) with

\lambda=\lambda_{c}+\epsilon
associated with principal eigenvalue
\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume (H1)-(H8). Let
U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
be a pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
)
with
c>c_{+}^{0}
, then there exists
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\begin{aligned} \sup_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}}\frac{U(x,s)-\delta\bm{\xi}(x,s+z_{0})-\bm{1}}{\bm{\Psi}(x)}&\leq-\frac{\delta}{2}\bm{1},\end{aligned}\quad\forall\;\delta\in(0,\delta_{m}].

(5.5)

Proof.

We first prove that

\limsup_{z\to+\infty}\left\{\sup_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R},\ \delta\in(0,\delta_{m}]}\frac{U(x,s)-\delta\bm{\xi}(x,s+z)-\bm{1}}{\delta\bm{\Psi}(x)}\right\}\leq-\bm{1}.

(5.6)

If (
5.6
) is not true, then there exist
\{(x_{n},s_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,

\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{z_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

z_{n}\to+\infty\ (n\to\infty),\quad\delta_{n}\in(0,\delta_{m}],\qquad\frac{U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n})-\delta_{n}\xi_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+z_{n})-1}{\delta_{n}\psi_{i}(x_{n})}\geq-1+\tau

for some
i\in I
and
\tau\in(0,1)
.
Observe that
U_{i}
,
\xi_{i}
and
\psi_{i}
are periodic in
x
,
one may assume without loss of generality that
x_{n}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
,
and hence
x_{n}\to x_{*}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
as
n\to\infty
up to a subsequence.
Since
z_{n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
, we have either
s_{n}+z_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty

or
\{s_{n}+z_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded from above.
If
s_{n}+z_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, then by the definition of
\xi_{i}
, we have

-1=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{-\delta_{n}\xi_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+z_{n})}{\delta_{n}\psi_{i}(x_{n})}\geq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n})-\delta_{n}\xi_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+z_{n})-1}{\delta_{n}\psi_{i}(x_{n})}\geq-1+\tau,

which is a contradiction.
Therefore
\{s_{n}+z_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded from above, and thus
s_{n}\to-\infty
as
n\to\infty
.
Noting that
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{s\to-\infty}U_{i}(x,s)=0
uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi_{i}\geq 0
, then

-\frac{1}{\psi_{i}(x_{*})}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n})-1}{\psi_{i}(x_{n})}\geq\lim_{n\to\infty}(-1+\tau)\delta_{n}\geq(-1+\tau)\delta_{m},

which contradicts the definition of
\delta_{m}
.
Hence (
5.6
) holds, and it follows from (
5.6
) that
there exists
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that (
5.5
) holds.
The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Assume (H1)-(H8).
Let
\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
be a pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
)
with
c>c_{+}^{0}
.
Then there exists
\delta_{c}\in(0,\delta_{m}]
such that for any
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\delta\in(0,\delta_{c}]
and
\sigma\geq\frac{1}{\beta}
,
the functions
\bm{u}^{\pm}(t,x)
defined by

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\pm}(t,x)

\displaystyle=U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))\pm\delta\bm{\xi}(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}+z_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))e^{-\beta t}

are super- and subsolutions of (
1.4
) in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
respectively, where
z_{0}
is given by Lemma
5.3
.

Proof.

We only prove that
\bm{u}^{-}
is a subsolution since the other one can be proved similarly.
Let
\hat{s}=ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}-\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t})
and
\check{s}=\hat{s}+z_{0}
.
Then

\bm{u}^{-}(t,x)=U(x,\hat{s})-\delta\bm{\xi}(x,\check{s})e^{-\beta t}=\bm{u}\left(\frac{\hat{s}+x\cdot e}{c},x\right)-\delta\bm{\xi}(x,\check{s})e^{-\beta t}.

For each
i
, by a direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial u_{i}^{-}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i}^{-}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{-}-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})

\displaystyle=f_{i}(x,\bm{u})-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})+\delta\beta\xi_{i}e^{-\beta t}-\frac{\sigma\beta}{c}\frac{\partial u_{i}}{\partial t}e^{-\beta t}

\displaystyle\quad-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\chi e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}[-d_{i}\Delta\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-(q_{i}-2d_{i}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)e)\cdot\nabla\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 108.12054pt-\left(d_{i}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)^{2}+(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)q_{i}\cdot e+c(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\right)\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt-(1-\chi)[d_{i}\Delta\psi_{i}+q_{i}\cdot\nabla\psi_{i}]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}[\chi^{\prime}(c-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t})\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-\chi(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 108.12054pt+2d_{i}\chi^{\prime}\nabla\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\cdot e-d_{i}\chi^{\prime\prime}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-2d_{i}\chi^{\prime}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}+q_{i}\cdot e\chi^{\prime}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt-\chi^{\prime}(c-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t})\psi_{i}-2d_{i}\chi^{\prime}\nabla\psi_{i}\cdot e+d_{i}\chi^{\prime\prime}\psi_{i}-q_{i}\cdot e\chi^{\prime}\psi_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}(\delta\xi_{j}e^{-\beta t})+(h_{i}(x,\bm{u})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-}))u_{i}+\delta\xi_{i}e^{-\beta t}h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})+\delta\beta\xi_{i}e^{-\beta t}-\frac{\sigma\beta}{c}\frac{\partial u_{i}}{\partial t}e^{-\beta t}

\displaystyle\quad-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\chi e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\left[\left(c(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)-\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\right)\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j}^{\epsilon}+h_{i}(x,\bm{0})\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 62.59596pt-(1-\chi)\left[\mu^{-}\psi_{i}-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})\psi_{k}\right]+R(x,\check{s})\right\}

\displaystyle=-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\xi_{j}-u_{i}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,s\bm{u}+(1-s)\bm{u}^{-})}ds\right)}\xi_{k}\right]-\xi_{i}h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{\sigma\beta}{\delta}\frac{\partial U_{i}}{\partial s}+\chi e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\left[|\sigma_{\epsilon}|\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j}^{\epsilon}+h_{i}(x,\bm{0})\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-\beta\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt-(1-\chi)\left[\mu^{-}\psi_{i}-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})\psi_{k}+\beta\psi_{i}\right]+R(x,\check{s})\right\}

\displaystyle=-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\frac{\sigma\beta}{\delta}\frac{\partial U_{i}}{\partial s}+\chi e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\left[\beta\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}+(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-}))\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-u_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}{h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\phi_{k}^{\epsilon}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+(1-\chi)\left[-(\mu^{-}+\beta)\psi_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})\psi_{k}-u_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}{h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\psi_{k}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 108.12054pt-\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\psi_{j}-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})\psi_{i}\right]+R(x,\check{s})\right\}

\displaystyle=-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\frac{\sigma\beta}{\delta}\frac{\partial U_{i}}{\partial s}+\chi e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\left[\beta\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}+(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-}))\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-u_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}{h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\phi_{k}^{\epsilon}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+(1-\chi)\Bigg[-(\mu^{-}+\beta)\psi_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})-u_{i}h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\right)\psi_{k}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 110.96556pt+(h_{i}(x,\bm{1})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-}))\psi_{i}\Bigg]+R(x,\check{s})\right\},

where
a_{ij}\equiv 0
if
i=1
, and

h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta):=\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,s\bm{u}+(1-s)\bm{u}^{-})}ds=\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}\left(x,\bm{u}-(1-s)\delta\bm{\xi}e^{-\beta t}\right)}ds,

and

\displaystyle R(x,\check{s})

\displaystyle:=e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}[\chi^{\prime}(c-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t})\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-\chi(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+2d_{i}\chi^{\prime}\nabla\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\cdot e-d_{i}\chi^{\prime\prime}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-2d_{i}\chi^{\prime}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}+q_{i}\cdot e\chi^{\prime}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}]

\displaystyle\qquad-\chi^{\prime}(c-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t})\psi_{i}-2d_{i}\chi^{\prime}\nabla\psi_{i}\cdot e+d_{i}\chi^{\prime\prime}\psi_{i}-q_{i}\cdot e\chi^{\prime}\psi_{i}.

Let

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)

\displaystyle=|h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})|+\sum_{k=1}^{m}|u_{i}h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)

\displaystyle=|h_{i}(x,\bm{1})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})|+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left|\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})-u_{i}h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\right|.

Noting that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{\hat{s}\to-\infty}\Gamma_{0}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)=0
uniformly
in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
In view of Theorem
1.11
and Corollary
3.9
, there exists
M_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial U_{i}(x,s)}{\partial s}\geq\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{i}}{2}U_{i}(x,s)\geq\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{i}\rho}{4}e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{i}^{c}(x),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-M_{0}],

and

e^{-\epsilon M_{0}}\leq\min_{i\in I}\left\{\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{i}\rho\min_{x}\phi_{i}^{c}(x)}{4\delta_{m}(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\max_{x}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}(x)}\right\}.

(5.7)

Moreover,
since
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{\hat{s}\to+\infty}U(x,\hat{s})=\bm{1}
uniformly
in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
, there exists
M_{1}\geq M_{0}
such that

\displaystyle 0\leq\Gamma_{1}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left|\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,s\bm{1}+(1-s)\bm{u}^{-})\right||1-u_{k}+\delta\psi_{k}e^{-\beta t}|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left|\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})-\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}\left(x,\bm{u}-(1-s)\delta\bm{\xi}e^{-\beta t}\right)}ds\right|

\displaystyle\quad+|1-u_{i}|\sum_{k=1}^{m}\int_{0}^{1}{\left|\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}\left(x,\bm{u}-(1-s)\delta\bm{\xi}e^{-\beta t}\right)\right|}ds

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}\delta(1+|\bm{\Psi}|)+K_{2}\delta(1+2|\bm{\Psi}|)+\delta mK_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\delta K

for all
(x,\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[M_{1},\infty)
,
where
K=K_{1}(1+m+|\bm{\Psi}|)+K_{2}(1+2|\bm{\Psi}|)
, with

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\max_{k\in I}\left\{\max_{(x,\bm{u})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-\theta\bm{1},\theta\bm{1}]}\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{u})\right\},

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{k,l\in I}\left\{\max_{(x,\bm{u})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-\theta\bm{1},\theta\bm{1}]}\frac{\partial^{2}h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}\partial u_{l}}(x,\bm{u})\right\},\quad\theta=1+\delta_{m}|\bm{\Psi}|.

Therefore, there exist
M\geq M_{1}
with
M>\bar{s}
and
-M<\underline{s}
,
and
\delta_{0}\leq\delta_{m}
such that for any
0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\min_{k\in I}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{k}^{\epsilon}(x)\right\},\qquad\forall\;(x,\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-M],

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|\bm{\Psi}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\min_{k\in I}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\psi_{k}(x)\right\},\qquad\forall\;(x,\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[M,+\infty).

Consequently:

(i) For any
(x,\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-M]
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial u_{i}^{-}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i}^{-}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{-}-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})

\displaystyle\leq-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\frac{\sigma\beta}{\delta}\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{i}\rho}{4}e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{i}^{c}+e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\frac{\beta}{2}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t}e^{\lambda_{c}\check{s}}\left\{\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{i}\rho}{4}\phi_{i}^{c}-\delta(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)e^{-\beta t}e^{\epsilon\check{s}}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 0,

where the last inequality follows from (
5.7
).

(ii) For any
(x,\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[M,+\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial u_{i}^{-}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i}^{-}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{-}-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})

\displaystyle\leq-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left[-(\mu^{-}+\beta)\psi_{i}-\Gamma_{1}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|\bm{\Psi}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\delta e^{-\beta t}\psi_{i}\left[-(\mu^{-}+\beta)-\frac{\beta}{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 0,

where we used the fact that
\beta=\frac{|\sigma_{\epsilon}|}{2}\leq\frac{|\mu^{-}|}{2}
.

(iii) For any
(x,\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-M,M]
, let

\triangle_{i}(x,\hat{s})=e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\Gamma_{0}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|+\Gamma_{1}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|\bm{\Psi}|+|R(x,\check{s})|,

and define

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}

\displaystyle=\left\{\inf_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-M,M]}\frac{\partial U_{i}(x,s)}{\partial s}\right\}>0,

\displaystyle\delta_{c}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\delta_{m},\delta_{0},\frac{\alpha_{i}}{\mathop{\sup}\limits_{(x,\hat{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-M,M]}|\triangle_{i}(x,\hat{s})|}\right\}>0.

Noting that
\sigma\beta\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial u_{i}^{-}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i}^{-}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{-}-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})

\displaystyle\leq-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\frac{\sigma\beta}{\delta}\alpha_{i}-e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\check{s}}\Gamma_{0}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|-\Gamma_{1}^{i}(x,\hat{s};\delta)|\bm{\Psi}|-|R(x,\check{s})|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t}\left(\alpha_{i}-\delta_{c}\triangle_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 0.

By (i)-(iii), we conclude that
\bm{u}^{-}
is a subsolution of (
1.4
) in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
Using a similarly argument, one can prove that
\bm{u}^{+}
is a subsolution in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
. The proof is complete.
∎

In the following of this subsection, for any
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, we denote

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\pm}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0})

\displaystyle=U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))\pm\delta_{c}\bm{\xi}(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}+z_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))e^{-\beta t}.

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume (H1)-(H8). Let
\bm{0}<\bm{u}_{0}<\bm{1}
satisfy
(
5.2
) for some
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\frac{\delta_{c}}{2\delta_{M}})

and (
5.3
) with
\tau=0
, where
\delta_{c}>0
is given in Lemma
5.4
.
Then there exist
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\sigma_{c}\geq 1
and
t_{c}>0
such that for any
\sigma\geq\sigma_{c}
,

\bm{u}^{-}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0})\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{u}^{+}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in[t_{c},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Proof.

Let
T(t)={\rm diag}(T_{i}(t))_{i\in I}
be the operator defined on
Y
,
and
T_{i}(t)
is the linear semigroup generated by

w_{t}=d_{i}(x)\Delta w+q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla w
,
i\in I
.
Then

\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})=T(t)\bm{u}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{T(t-s)\bm{F}(x,\bm{u}(s,x;\bm{u}_{0}))ds},\quad t>0.

Noting that
\lim_{t\to 0}|\bm{u}(t,\cdot;\bm{u}_{0})-\bm{u}_{0}|=0
, and for any
t>0
, there hold

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varsigma\to+\infty}\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\geq\varsigma\end{subarray}}(\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})-\bm{1})\geq\liminf_{\varsigma\to+\infty}\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\geq\varsigma\end{subarray}}(\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})-\bm{u}_{0}(x))+\liminf_{\varsigma\to+\infty}\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\geq\varsigma\end{subarray}}(\bm{u}_{0}(x)-\bm{1}).

Since there exists
\gamma>1
such that

0\leq\gamma\varepsilon_{0}\leq\frac{\delta_{c}}{2\delta_{M}}
,
it follows from (
5.2
) that there exists
t_{c}>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varsigma\to+\infty}\left\{\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\geq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\frac{\bm{u}(t_{c},x;\bm{u}_{0})-\bm{1}}{\bm{\Psi}(x)}\right\}\gg-\delta_{M}\gamma\varepsilon_{0}e^{-\beta t_{c}}\bm{1}.

It then follows from Lemma
5.3
that

\displaystyle\sup_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}}\frac{U(x,s)-\delta_{c}\bm{\xi}(x,s+z_{0})e^{-\beta t_{c}}-\bm{1}}{\bm{\Psi}(x)}

\displaystyle\ll\liminf_{\varsigma\to+\infty}\left\{\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\geq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\frac{\bm{u}(t_{c},x;\bm{u}_{0})-\bm{1}}{\bm{\psi}(x)}\right\}.

(5.8)

By Theorem
1.11
, there exists
s_{0}=s_{0}(k)
such that

\limsup_{\varsigma\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\leq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{U(x,-x\cdot e+s_{0})}{ke^{-\lambda_{c}(x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)}-\bm{1}\right|\right\}=0.

(5.9)

Next, we prove that there exists
\sigma_{1}\geq 1
such that
