\bm{u}^{-}_{\sigma}(t_{c},x,s_{0})\leq\bm{u}(t_{c},x;\bm{u}_{0}),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\quad\forall\;\sigma\geq\sigma_{1}.

Assume this is not true, then there exist
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

and
\{\sigma_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\sigma_{n}\to+\infty\ (n\to\infty),\quad u^{-}_{i,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})>u_{i}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})

for some
i\in I
.
Let
s_{n}=ct_{c}-x_{n}\cdot e+s_{0}-\sigma_{n}(1-e^{-\beta t_{c}})
.
If
\{s_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded from below,
then
-x_{n}\cdot e\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
,
and it follows from (
5.8
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{u^{-}_{i,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})-1}{\psi_{i}(x_{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n})-\delta_{c}\xi_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+z_{0})e^{-\beta t_{c}}-1}{\psi_{i}(x_{n})}

\displaystyle<\liminf_{n\to\infty}\left\{\frac{u_{i}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})-1}{\psi_{i}(x_{n})}\right\},

which is a contradiction. Therefore
s_{n}\to-\infty
as
n\to\infty
.
Noting that

0=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}u^{-}_{i,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})\geq\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}u_{i}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})\geq 0,

then
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}u_{i}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})=0
.
Now if
\{x_{n}\cdot e\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded, we write
x_{n}=x_{n}^{\prime}+x_{n}^{\prime\prime}
, where
x_{n}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}
and
x_{n}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
with

x_{n}^{\prime\prime}\to x_{\infty}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
as
n\to\infty
.
Let

\bm{u}_{n}(t,x)=\bm{u}(t,x+x_{n}^{\prime};\bm{u}_{0}).

Then
\bm{u}_{n}
solves (
5.1
), and
\bm{0}\leq\bm{u}_{n}\leq\bm{1}
for any
(t,x)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
Up to an extraction of subsequence, we assume that
\bm{u}_{n}
converges locally uniformly in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
to
\bm{u}_{\infty}\geq\bm{0}
, which solves (
5.1
),
and in particular,
u_{i,\infty}
satisfies

\frac{\partial u_{i,\infty}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i,\infty}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i,\infty}-\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{i}}(x,\tau\bm{u}_{\infty})}d\tau\right)u_{i,\infty}\geq 0.

Observe that
u_{i,\infty}(t_{c},x_{\infty})=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}u_{i,n}(t_{c},x_{n}^{\prime\prime})=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}u_{i}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})=0
.
It then follows from the maximum principle that
u_{i,\infty}(t,x)\equiv 0
for any
(t,x)\in(0,t_{c}]\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
On the other hand, since
\{x_{n}\cdot e\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded, so is
\{x_{n}^{\prime}\cdot e\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
and then we obtain from (
5.2
) that

\liminf_{\varsigma\to+\infty}\left\{\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ -x\cdot e\geq\varsigma}\bm{u}_{\infty}(0,x)\right\}\geq(1-\varepsilon_{0})\bm{1}.

Hence there exists

\hat{x}\in B_{\frac{R}{2}}(0):=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:|x|\leq\frac{R}{2}\right\}

with
R>0
such that

\bm{u}_{\infty}(0,\hat{x})\geq\frac{(1-\varepsilon_{0})\bm{1}}{2}\gg\bm{0}.

Notice that
\bm{u}_{\infty}\geq\bm{0}
for any
(t,x)\in(0,t_{c}+1)\times\mathring{B}_{R}(0)
,
and
\bm{u}_{\infty}(0,x)>\bm{0}
for any
x\in B_{R}(0)
,
where
\mathring{B}_{R}(0):=\left\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}:|x|<R\right\}
.
Then
\bm{u}_{\infty}(t_{c},x)\gg\bm{0}
for any
x\in B_{\frac{R}{2}}(0)

due to the maximum principle, which is a contradiction since
u_{i,\infty}\equiv 0
in
(0,t_{c}]\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
As a result,
-x_{n}\cdot e\to-\infty
as
n\to\infty
.
Consequently,

0=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{u^{-}_{i,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})}{U_{i}(x_{n},ct_{c}-x_{n}\cdot e+s_{0})}\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{u_{i}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})}{U_{i}(x_{n},ct_{c}-x_{n}\cdot e+s_{0})}=1,

where the left-hand equality follows from Theorem
1.11

and the right-hand equality follows from Lemma
5.2
.
This contradiction shows that there exists
\sigma_{1}\geq 1
such that

\bm{u}^{-}_{\sigma}(t_{c},x,s_{0})\leq\bm{u}(t_{c},x;\bm{u}_{0})
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}

and
\sigma\geq\sigma_{1}
.

Now, we prove that there exists
\sigma_{2}\geq 1
such that

\bm{u}(t_{c},x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{u}^{+}_{\sigma}(t_{c},x,s_{0})
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\sigma\geq\sigma_{2}
.
Again we argue by a contradiction. If this is not true, then there exist
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\sigma_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\sigma_{n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
and

u^{+}_{j,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})<u_{j}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})
for some
j\in I
.
Denote
z_{n}=ct_{c}-x_{n}\cdot e+s_{0}+\sigma_{n}(1-e^{-\beta t_{c}})
.
If
z_{n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
, then

1\geq\limsup_{n\to\infty}u_{j}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}u^{+}_{j,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})\geq 1+\delta_{c}e^{-\beta t_{c}}\min_{x}\psi_{j}(x)>1.

This contradiction shows that
\{z_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded from above.
Hence
-x_{n}\cdot e\to-\infty
as
n\to\infty
, and it follows from Lemma
5.2
that

0\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}u^{+}_{j,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}u_{j}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})=\lim_{n\to\infty}U_{j}(x_{n},ct_{c}-x_{n}\cdot e+s_{0})=0,

which together with the fact that
\xi_{j}\geq 0
yields that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{n\to\infty}z_{n}=-\infty
.
Observe that

\infty=\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{u^{+}_{j,\sigma_{n}}(t_{c},x_{n},s_{0})}{U_{j}(x_{n},ct_{c}-x_{n}\cdot e+s_{0})}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{u_{j}(t_{c},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})}{U(x_{n},ct_{c}-x_{n}\cdot e+s_{0})}=1,

this contradiction shows that
\bm{u}(t_{c},x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{u}^{+}_{\sigma}(t_{c},x,s_{0})

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\sigma\geq\sigma_{2}
.

To this end, let
\sigma_{c}=\max\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\}
,
then for any
\sigma\geq\sigma_{c}
,

\bm{u}^{-}_{\sigma}(t_{c},x,s_{0})\leq\bm{u}(t_{c},x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{u}^{+}_{\sigma}(t_{c},x,s_{0}),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Noting that
\bm{u}^{-}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0})\leq\bm{1}
and

\bm{u}^{+}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0})>\bm{0}
, and

\bm{0}\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{1}
for all
(t,x)\in[t_{c},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
It then follows from the maximum principle that

\bm{u}^{-}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0})\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{u}^{+}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in[t_{c},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Suppose that all the assumptions in Lemma
5.5
are satisfied.
Let
\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
be a pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
)
with
c>c_{+}^{0}
.
Then for any
\eta>0
, there exist
D_{\eta}>0
and
s_{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle U(x,ct-x\cdot e-\eta)-D_{\eta}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})

and

\displaystyle\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq U(x,ct-x\cdot e+\eta)+D_{\eta}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ ct-x\cdot e\leq s_{\eta}\}
.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that
s_{0}=0
, where
s_{0}
is given by (
5.9
).
For any
\eta>0
, it follows from (
5.2
) that

\limsup_{\varsigma\to-\infty}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\leq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{U(x,-x\cdot e-\eta)}{\bm{u}_{0}(x)}\right|\leq\bm{1}.

Hence there exists
M>0
such that
U(x,-x\cdot e-\eta)\leq\bm{u}_{0}(x)
for any

x\in\{\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ -x\cdot e\leq-M\}
.
Since
\mathop{\inf}\limits_{-x\cdot e\geq-M}\bm{u}_{0}(x)\geq\bm{0}
,
there exists
D_{0}=D_{0}(\eta)>0
such that for any
D\geq D_{0}
,

\displaystyle U(x,-x\cdot e-\eta)-De^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)\leq\bm{u}_{0}(x)

for any
x\in\{\mathbb{R}^{N}:-x\cdot e\geq-M\}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle U(x,-x\cdot e-\eta)-De^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)\leq\bm{u}_{0}(x),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\ \ \forall\;D\geq D_{0}.

Let

m_{0}=\min_{i\in I}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}(x)}{\phi_{i}^{c}(x)}\right\},\qquad m_{\epsilon}=\min_{i\in I}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}(x)\right\}

and

K_{1}=\max_{i,k=1,2,\cdots,m}\left\{\max_{(x,\bm{u})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-\bm{1},\bm{1}]}\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{u})\right\}.

Observe that there exists
\tau\in(0,1)
such that for any
|\bm{u}|\leq\tau
,

\displaystyle|\bm{u}|\leq\frac{|\sigma_{\epsilon}|m_{\epsilon}}{2(K_{1}+1)|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|},\qquad|h_{i}(x,\bm{u})-h_{i}(x,\bm{0})|\leq\frac{|\sigma_{\epsilon}|m_{\epsilon}}{2(K_{1}+1)|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|}.

By Theorem
1.11
, there exists
s_{0}=s_{0}(\eta)<0
such that

U(x,s-\eta)\leq\frac{3\rho}{2}e^{\lambda_{c}s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,s_{0}].

Let
s_{1}\leq s_{0}
be such that

3\rho e^{\lambda_{c}s_{1}}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}|\leq\tau,\quad\frac{3\rho}{m_{0}}e^{\lambda_{c}s_{1}}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|\leq\tau,

and set

D_{\eta}^{-}=\max\left\{D_{0},e^{-(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s_{1}}\right\}
.
Then
\underline{s}_{\eta}:=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\ln\frac{3\rho}{2m_{0}D_{\eta}^{-}}\leq s_{1}

for
s_{1}
small enough. Define

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)=\bm{u}\left(t-\frac{\eta}{c},x\right)-D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x).

It is easy to see that for all
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}_{\eta}\}
,

\left|\bm{u}\left(t-\frac{\eta}{c},x\right)\right|=|U(x,s-\eta)|\leq\frac{3\rho}{2}e^{\lambda_{c}s}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}|\leq\frac{\tau}{2}

and

|\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)|\leq\left|\bm{u}\left(t-\frac{\eta}{c},x\right)\right|+D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e)}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|\leq\frac{\tau}{2}+\frac{3\rho}{2m_{0}}e^{\lambda_{c}\underline{s}_{\eta}}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|\leq\tau.

Moreover, for any

(t,x)\in\left\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e=\underline{s}_{\eta}\right\}
,

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)

\displaystyle=U(x,\underline{s}_{\eta}-\eta)-D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\underline{s}_{\eta}}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\rho e^{\lambda_{c}\underline{s}_{\eta}}\left(\frac{3}{2}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}}(x)-\frac{D_{\eta}^{-}}{\rho}e^{\epsilon\underline{s}_{\eta}}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)\right)\leq\bm{0}.

Observe that

\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}=(\underline{u}_{1,\eta},\underline{u}_{2,\eta},\cdots,\underline{u}_{m,\eta})
satisfies

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial\underline{u}_{i,\eta}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta\underline{u}_{i,\eta}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla\underline{u}_{i,\eta}-f_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})

\displaystyle=f_{i}(x,\bm{u})-f_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})-D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j}^{\epsilon}+(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})+|\sigma_{\epsilon}|)\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right]

\displaystyle=u_{i}[h_{i}(x,\bm{u})-h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})]+D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s}\left(h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})-h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-|\sigma_{\epsilon}|\right)\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s}\left\{u_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,s\bm{u}+(1-s)\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})ds}\right)\phi_{k}^{\epsilon}+(h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})-h_{i}(x,\bm{0}))\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}-|\sigma_{\epsilon}|\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s}\left\{(K_{1}|\bm{u}|+|h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})-h_{i}(x,\bm{0})|)|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}|-|\sigma_{\epsilon}|m_{\epsilon}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 0,\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}_{\eta}\},\ \quad i\in I,

where
a_{ij}\equiv 0
if
i=1
.
Consequently,
\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}
is a subsolution of (
1.4
)
in
\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}_{\eta}\}
.
It then follows from Lemma
5.1
that

U(x,s-\eta)-D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}_{\eta}\}
.

Similarly, it can be shown that there exists
D_{1}>0
such that for any
D\geq D_{1}
, there holds

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{0}(x)\leq U(x,-x\cdot e+\eta)+De^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x),\quad\forall\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Observe that

\mathop{\lim}\limits_{-x\cdot e\to-\infty}\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})=\bm{0}

uniformly in
t\in[0,t_{c}]
by Lemma
5.2
, and

\displaystyle\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq U(x,ct-x\cdot e+\sigma_{c}(1-e^{-\beta t}))+\delta_{c}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e+z_{0}+\sigma_{c}(1-e^{-\beta t}))}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)e^{-\beta t}

for any

(t,x)\in\left\{[t_{c},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}-z_{0}-\sigma_{c}\right\}

in terms of Lemma
5.5
,
we only need to consider for each
\eta\leq\sigma_{c}
.
Let

M_{\epsilon}=\max_{i\in I}\left\{\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{i}^{\epsilon}(x)\right\},\qquad\theta_{\epsilon}=\frac{m_{\epsilon}}{M_{\epsilon}}.

In view of Lemma
5.2
, there exists
s_{2}\leq 0
such that

|\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})|\leq\frac{\tau\theta_{\epsilon}}{2},\quad|U(x,ct-x\cdot e+\eta)|\leq\frac{\tau\theta_{\epsilon}}{2},\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq s_{2}\}.

Let

\bar{s}_{\eta}:=\min\left\{s_{2},\frac{1}{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}\ln\frac{\tau}{2D_{1}M_{\epsilon}}\right\},\qquad D_{\eta}^{+}:=\max\left\{D_{1},\frac{\tau e^{-(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)\bar{s}_{\eta}}}{2M_{\epsilon}}\right\},

and define

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)=\bm{u}\left(t+\frac{\eta}{c},x\right)+D_{\eta}^{+}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x).

One can easily obtain that

|\overline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)|\leq\tau

for all
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\bar{s}_{\eta}\}
,
and

\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\frac{\tau\theta_{\epsilon}}{2}\leq\overline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e=\bar{s}_{\eta}\}.

Furthermore, a similar argument as above shows that

\overline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}
is a supersolution of (
1.4
) in

\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\bar{s}_{\eta}\}
.
Lemma
5.1
again implies that

\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq U(x,s+\eta)+D_{\eta}^{+}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:ct-x\cdot e\leq\bar{s}_{\eta}\}
.
Let
s_{\eta}=\min\{\underline{s}_{\eta},\bar{s}_{\eta}\}
and
D_{\eta}=\max\{D_{\eta}^{-},D_{\eta}^{+}\}
,
then the proof is complete.
∎

5.2.
The critical case
c=c_{+}^{0}(e)

In this subsection, we consider the critical case
c_{*}=c_{+}^{0}(e)
.
Recall that
\lambda_{*}>0
is such that
\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})=c_{*}\lambda_{*}
, and

\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)=\left(\phi_{1,*}(x),\phi_{2,*}(x),\cdots,\phi_{m,*}(x)\right)

is the positive periodic eigenfunction of (
3.16
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{*}

associated with the principal eigenvalue
\kappa=\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*})
.
Let
\epsilon_{*}>0
be a fixed constant satisfying (
3.17
) and

|\sigma_{*}|\leq\frac{|\mu^{-}|}{2}
, and

\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)=\left(\phi_{1,\epsilon_{*}}(x),\phi_{2,\epsilon_{*}}(x),\cdots,\phi_{m,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

be the positive periodic eigenfunction of (
3.16
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}

associated with
\kappa=\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})
,
where

\sigma_{*}=c_{*}(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})-\kappa_{1}(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})<0.

Let
\chi(s)
be defined by (
5.4
), and define

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{*}(x,s)

\displaystyle=\chi(s)e^{\lambda_{*}s}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)+(1-\chi(s))\bm{\Psi}(x).

Lemma 5.7
.

Assume (H1)-(H8). Let
U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)
be the critical pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
). Then there exists
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\sup_{(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}}\frac{U(x,s)-\delta\bm{\xi}_{*}(x,s+z_{0})-\bm{1}}{\bm{\Psi}(x)}\leq-\frac{\delta}{2}\bm{1},\quad\forall\;\delta\in(0,\delta_{m}].

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
5.3
, we omit it here.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Assume (H1)-(H8).
Let
\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)
be the critical pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
).
Then there exists
\delta_{*}\in(0,\delta_{m}]
such that for any
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\delta\in(0,\delta_{*}]
and
\sigma\geq\frac{1}{\beta}
, where
\beta:=|\sigma_{*}|
,
the functions
\bm{u}^{\pm}(t,x)
defined by

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\pm}(t,x)

\displaystyle=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))\pm\delta\bm{\xi}_{*}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{0}+z_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))e^{-\beta t}

are super- and subsolutions of (
1.4
) in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
,
where
z_{0}
is given by Lemma
5.7
.

Proof.

We only give a sketch here since the proof is similar to that of Lemma
5.4
.
Let
\hat{s}=c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{0}-\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t})
and
\check{s}=\hat{s}+z_{0}
,
then

\bm{u}^{-}(t,x)=U(x,\hat{s})-\delta\bm{\xi}_{*}(x,\hat{s}+z_{0})e^{-\beta t}
,
and for each
i
, a direct calculation shows that

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial u_{i}^{-}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i}^{-}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i}^{-}-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})

\displaystyle=f_{i}(x,\bm{u})-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-})-\frac{\sigma\beta}{c}\frac{\partial u_{i}}{\partial t}e^{-\beta t}

\displaystyle\quad-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\chi e^{\lambda_{*}\check{s}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j,*}+(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-\beta)\phi_{i,*}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 62.59596pt-\chi e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})\check{s}}\left[\sigma_{*}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j,\epsilon_{*}}+(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-\beta)\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 62.59596pt-(1-\chi)\left[\mu^{-}\psi_{i}+\beta\psi_{i}-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})\psi_{k}\right]+R_{*}(x,\check{s})\right\}

\displaystyle=-\delta e^{-\beta t}\left\{\frac{\sigma\beta}{\delta}\frac{\partial U_{i}}{\partial s}+\chi e^{\lambda_{*}\check{s}}\left[(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-}))\phi_{i,*}-u_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\phi_{k,*}-\beta\phi_{i,*}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt-\chi e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})\check{s}}\left[(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-}))\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}-u_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\phi_{k,\epsilon_{*}}-2\beta\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+(1-\chi)\Bigg[-(\mu^{-}+\beta)\psi_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})-u_{i}h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)\right)\psi_{k}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 110.96556pt+(h_{i}(x,\bm{1})-h_{i}(x,\bm{u}^{-}))\psi_{i}\Bigg]+R_{*}(x,\check{s})\right\},

where
a_{ij}\equiv 0
if
i=1
, and

h_{i,k}(x,\hat{s};\delta)=\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,s\bm{u}+(1-s)\bm{u}^{-})}ds=\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}\left(x,\bm{u}-(1-s)\delta\bm{\xi}_{*}e^{-\beta t}\right)}ds,

and

\displaystyle R_{*}(x,\check{s})=

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}e^{\lambda_{*}\check{s}}\left[(c-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t}+q_{i}\cdot e)(\phi_{i,*}-e^{\epsilon_{*}\check{s}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}})+2d_{i}(\nabla\phi_{i,*}-e^{\epsilon_{*}\check{s}}\nabla\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}})\cdot e\right]

\displaystyle-\chi^{\prime}[(c-\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t})\psi_{i}+2d_{i}\nabla\psi_{i}\cdot e+q_{i}\cdot e\psi_{i}]+d_{i}\chi^{\prime\prime}[\psi_{i}-e^{\lambda_{*}\check{s}}(\phi_{i,*}-e^{\epsilon_{*}\check{s}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}})]

\displaystyle-\chi e^{\lambda_{*}\check{s}}\sigma\beta e^{-\beta t}[\lambda_{*}\phi_{i,*}-(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})e^{\epsilon_{*}\check{s}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}].

Noting from Theorem
1.11
and Corollary
3.9
that
for each
i
, there exists
M>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial U_{i}(x,s)}{\partial s}\geq\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{i}}{2}U_{i}(x,s)\geq\frac{\underline{\lambda}_{i}\rho}{4}|s|e^{\lambda_{c}s}\phi_{i}^{c}(x),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,-M].

By following the same line to the proof of Lemma
5.4
, one can prove that

\bm{u}^{-}
is a subsolution of (
1.4
) in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
Similarly,
\bm{u}^{+}
is a supersolution of (
1.4
).
The proof is complete.
∎

In the following of this subsection, denote

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\pm}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0})

\displaystyle=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))\pm\delta_{*}\bm{\xi}_{*}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{0}+z_{0}\pm\sigma(1-e^{-\beta t}))e^{-\beta t}.

Lemma 5.9
.

Assume (H1)-(H8). Let
\bm{0}<\bm{u}_{0}<\bm{1}
satisfy
(
5.2
) for some
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,\frac{\delta_{*}}{2\delta_{M}})

and (
5.3
) with
\tau=1
, where
\delta_{*}>0
is given in Lemma
5.8
.
Then there exist
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\sigma_{*}\geq 1
and
t_{*}>0
such that for any
\sigma\geq\sigma_{*}
,

\bm{u}^{-}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0})\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq\bm{u}^{+}_{\sigma}(t,x,s_{0}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in[t_{*},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Proof.

By Theorem
1.11
, there exists
s_{0}=s_{0}(k)
such that

\limsup_{\varsigma\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\leq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{U(x,-x\cdot e+s_{0})}{k|x\cdot e|e^{-\lambda_{*}(x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)}-\bm{1}\right|\right\}=0.

(5.10)

The remaining of the proof is similar to that of Lemma
5.5
, we omit the details here.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Suppose that all the assumptions in Lemma
5.9
are satisfied.
Let
\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)
be the critical pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
).
Then for any
\eta>0
, there exist
D_{\eta}>0
and
s_{\eta}\leq\bar{s}
such that

\displaystyle U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e-\eta)-D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})

and

\displaystyle\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta)+D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\ |\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq s_{\eta}\}
.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that
s_{0}=0
, where
s_{0}
is given by (
5.10
).
Let
s_{*}\leq-1
be such that
\theta^{*}e^{\epsilon_{*}s}\leq\frac{1}{2}
for all
s\leq s_{*}
,
where
\theta^{*}=\max_{i\in I}\left\{\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\frac{\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x)}{\phi_{i,*}(x)}\right\}
.
By following similar arguments to those of Lemma
5.6
,
one can prove that there exists
D_{0}(\eta)>0
such that

\displaystyle U(x,-x\cdot e-\eta)-D_{0}e^{-\lambda_{*}(x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{-\epsilon_{*}(x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)\leq\bm{u}_{0}(x)

for any
x\in\{\mathbb{R}^{N}:-x\cdot e\leq s_{*}\}
.
In view of Theorem
1.11
, there exists
s_{1}\leq s_{*}
such that

\bm{0}<U(x,s)\leq\frac{3}{2}\rho|s|e^{\lambda_{*}s}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x),\quad\forall\;(x,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times(-\infty,s_{1}].

Denote

m_{\epsilon_{*}}=\min_{i\in I}\left\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right\},\quad K_{1}=\max_{i,k\in I}\left\{\max_{(x,\bm{u})\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\times[-\bm{1},\bm{1}]}\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{u})\right\}.

Let
\underline{s}_{\eta}\leq s_{1}
be such that

6\rho K_{1}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|^{2}|s|e^{(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s}\leq|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}},\quad\forall\;s\leq\underline{s}_{\eta},

and
D_{\eta}^{-}:=\max\{D_{0},3\rho|\underline{s}_{\eta}|\}
.
Define

\displaystyle\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)=\bm{u}\left(t-\frac{\eta}{c_{*}},x\right)-D_{\eta}^{-}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right).

It is easy to see that

\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)\leq\bm{0}
for any

(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e=\underline{s}_{\eta}\}
,
and

\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}(t,x)\leq\bm{1}
for any

(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}_{\eta}\}
.
Observe that

\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}=(\underline{u}_{1,\eta},\underline{u}_{2,\eta},\cdots,\underline{u}_{m,\eta}){{}^{T}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial\underline{u}_{i,\eta}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta\underline{u}_{i,\eta}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla\underline{u}_{i,\eta}-f_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})

\displaystyle=f_{i}(x,\bm{u})-f_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})-D_{\eta}^{-}\left[e^{\lambda_{*}s}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j,*}+h_{i}(x,\bm{0})\phi_{i,*}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 147.95424pt-e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}a_{ij}\phi_{j,\epsilon_{*}}+(h_{i}(x,\bm{0})-|\sigma_{*}|)\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=u_{i}(h_{i}(x,\bm{u})-h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta}))+D_{\eta}^{-}e^{\lambda_{*}s}(\phi_{i,*}-e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}})(h_{i}(x,\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})-h_{i}(x,\bm{0}))-D_{\eta}^{-}|\sigma_{*}|e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}

\displaystyle=D_{\eta}^{-}e^{\lambda_{*}s}\left\{u_{i}\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,s\bm{u}+(1-s)\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})ds}\right)(\phi_{k,*}-e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{k,\epsilon_{*}})}-|\sigma_{*}|e^{\epsilon_{*}s}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+(\phi_{i,*}-e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}})\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial h_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,s\underline{\bm{u}}_{\eta})ds}\right)(u_{k}-D_{\eta}^{-}e^{\lambda_{*}s}(\phi_{k,*}-e^{\lambda_{*}s}\phi_{k,\epsilon_{*}}))}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq D_{\eta}^{-}e^{\lambda_{*}s}\left\{K_{1}|\bm{u}||\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|-|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}}e^{\epsilon_{*}s}+K_{1}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|(|\bm{u}|+D_{\eta}^{-}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|e^{\lambda_{*}s})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\left\{(3\rho|s|+D_{\eta}^{-})K_{1}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|^{2}e^{(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s}-|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq D_{\eta}^{-}e^{(\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*})s}\left\{6\rho K_{1}|\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}|^{2}|s|e^{(\lambda_{*}-\epsilon_{*})s}-|\sigma_{*}|m_{\epsilon_{*}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 0,\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}_{\eta}\},\ \quad i=1,2,\cdots,m,

where
a_{ij}\equiv 0
if
i=1
. It then follows from Lemma
5.1
that

\displaystyle U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e-\eta)-D_{\eta}^{-}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)\leq\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\underline{s}_{\eta}\}
.
Similarly, one can prove that there exist
D_{\eta}^{+}>0
and
\bar{s}_{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta)+D_{\eta}^{+}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\bar{s}_{\eta}\}
.
Set
s_{\eta}=\min\{\underline{s}_{\eta},\bar{s}_{\eta}\}
and
D_{\eta}=\max\{D_{\eta}^{-},D_{\eta}^{+}\}
,
the proof is then complete.
∎

5.3.
Stability of pulsating traveling fronts

We prove the stability of pulsating traveling fronts in this subsection,
which is induced by the following lemma.

Lemma 5.11
.

Assume (H1)-(H8).
Let
U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
be a pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
) with
c\geq c_{+}^{0}
,
and
\bm{u}(t,x)
be a solution of (
1.4
) such that

U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}+\underline{s})\leq\bm{u}(t,x)\leq U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}+\overline{s}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}

for some
\underline{s}\leq 0\leq\overline{s}
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Moreover, for each
\eta>0
, there exist
D_{\eta}>0
and
s_{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ ct-x\cdot e\leq s_{\eta}\}
,

\displaystyle\quad U(x,ct-x\cdot e-\eta)-D_{\eta}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{u}(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq U(x,ct-x\cdot e+\eta)+D_{\eta}e^{(\lambda_{c}+\epsilon)(ct-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{c}+\epsilon}(x),\quad\text{if}\ \ c>c_{*}(e),

and

\displaystyle\quad U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e-\eta)-D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{u}(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta)+D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right),\quad\text{if}\ \ c=c_{*}(e).

Then

\bm{u}(t,x)=U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Proof.

We only prove for the case
c=c_{*}(e)
since the other one can be proved similarly.
Assume without loss of generality that
s_{0}=0
. Define

\bar{\eta}=\inf\left\{\eta\geq 0:\ U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta)\geq\bm{u}(t,x),\ \forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\right\}.

Then
0\leq\bar{\eta}\leq\overline{s}
.
Assume that
\bar{\eta}>0
, next we argue by a contradiction, which shows that
\bar{\eta}=0
.

Step 1
. We claim that there exists
\underline{z}\in(-\infty,s_{\bar{\eta}/2}]
such that

U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\bar{\eta}/2)\geq\bm{u}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\underline{z}\}.

(5.11)

Indeed, if this is not true, then there exists

\{(t_{n},x_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

s_{n}:=c_{*}t_{n}-x_{n}\cdot e\to-\infty\ (n\to\infty),\quad u_{i}(t_{n},x_{n})>U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+\bar{\eta}/2)

for some
i\in I
. In view of Theorem
1.11
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+\bar{\eta}/4)+D_{\bar{\eta}/4}e^{\lambda_{*}s_{n}}[\phi_{i,*}(x_{n})-e^{\epsilon_{*}s_{n}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x_{n})]}{U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+\bar{\eta}/2)}<1,

which together with the assumption shows that there exists
N

such that for all
n\geq N
,
s_{n}=c_{*}t_{n}-x_{n}\cdot e\leq s_{\bar{\eta}/4}
, and

\displaystyle u_{i}(t_{n},x_{n})

\displaystyle\leq U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+\bar{\eta}/4)+D_{\bar{\eta}/4}e^{\lambda_{*}s_{n}}\left(\phi_{i,*}(x_{n})-e^{\epsilon_{*}s_{n}}\phi_{i,\epsilon_{*}}(x_{n})\right){\leq U_{i}(x_{n},s_{n}+\bar{\eta}/2)}

which is a contradiction, and hence (
5.11
) holds.

Step 2
. We prove that

U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\bar{\eta})\gg\bm{u}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ \underline{z}\leq c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq z,\ \forall\;z\geq\underline{z}\}.

(5.12)

For any
z\geq\underline{z}
, assume to the contrary that there exists
i\in I
such that

\mathop{\inf}\limits_{\underline{z}\leq c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq z}\{U_{i}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\bar{\eta})-u_{i}(t,x)\}=0.

Then there exists
\{(t_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\underline{z}\leq s_{k}:=c_{*}t_{k}-x_{k}\cdot e\leq z,\qquad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{k\to\infty}\{U_{i}(x_{k},c_{*}t_{k}-x_{k}\cdot e+\bar{\eta})-u_{i}(t_{k},x_{k})\}=0.

Let
x_{k}=x_{k}^{\prime}+x_{k}^{\prime\prime}
with
x_{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}
and
x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
,
and assume (up to a subsequence) that
s_{k}\to s_{\infty}\in[\underline{z},z]
and

x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\to x_{\infty}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
as
k\to\infty
.
Let

\bm{u}_{k}(t,x)=\bm{u}(t+t_{k},x+x_{k}^{\prime}),

then
\bm{u}_{k}
solves (
1.4
) for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Up to an extraction of a subsequence,
\{\bm{u}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges
uniformly in any compact subset of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}

to a solution
\bm{u}_{\infty}\in[\bm{0},\bm{1}]
of (
1.4
).
Noting that

\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x)\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{\infty}+x_{\infty}\cdot e+\bar{\eta}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}

by the definition of
\bar{\eta}
. In particular,
u_{i,\infty}(0,x_{\infty})=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{k\to\infty}u_{i}(t_{k},x_{k})=U_{i}(x_{\infty},s_{\infty}+\bar{\eta})
.
Let

\bm{\omega}(t,x)=\bm{u}^{\bar{\eta}}(t,x)-\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x),\qquad\bm{u}^{\bar{\eta}}(t,x):=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{\infty}+x_{\infty}\cdot e+\bar{\eta}).

Then
\bm{\omega}\geq\bm{0}
, and
\omega_{i}(0,x_{\infty})=0
.
Observe that

\displaystyle(\omega_{i})_{t}-d_{i}\Delta\omega_{i}-q_{i}\cdot\nabla\omega_{i}\geq\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{i}}\left(x,\tau\bm{u}^{\bar{\eta}}+(1-\tau)\bm{u}_{\infty}\right)d\tau}\right)\omega_{i}.

It then follows from the maximum principle that

u_{i,\infty}(t,x)=U_{i}(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{\infty}+x_{\infty}\cdot e+\bar{\eta}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in(-\infty,0]\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

(5.13)

On the other hand, it follows from (
5.11
) that

\bm{u}_{k}(t,x)\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{k}+x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\cdot e+\bar{\eta}/2)

for all
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\underline{z}-s_{k}-x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\cdot e\}
.
By passing limits, one obtains that

\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x)\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{\infty}+x_{\infty}\cdot e+\bar{\eta}/2)

for all
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\underline{z}-s_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e\}
,
which contradicts (
5.13
). Hence (
5.12
) holds.

Step 3
. Noting from the assumptions that

\limsup_{\varsigma\to\infty}\left\{\sup_{c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\varsigma}|\bm{u}(t,x)-\bm{1}|\right\}=0.

Then there exists
\bar{z}>\underline{z}
such that

(1-\varrho^{*})\bm{1}\leq\bm{u}(t,x)\ll\bm{1},\qquad(1-\varrho^{*})\bm{1}\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)\ll\bm{1}

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}\}
,
where
\varrho^{*}=\min\{1,\min_{i\in I}\varrho_{i}\}
with
\varrho_{i}
given by (
4.1
).
In view of (
5.12
), there exists
\eta_{0}\in[\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2},\bar{\eta})
such that

U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta_{0})\geq\bm{u}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\underline{z}\leq c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\bar{z}\}.

(5.14)

Next we prove that

U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta_{0})\geq\bm{u}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}\}.

(5.15)

Let

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\theta}(t,x)=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta_{0})+\theta\bm{\Psi}(x)-\bm{u}(t,x),

and define

\bar{\theta}:=\inf\left\{\theta\geq 0\ |\ \bm{u}^{\theta}(t,x)\geq\bm{0},\ \forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}\}\right\}.

Observe that
\bar{\theta}\geq 0
is well defined, since

\min_{i\in I}\{\min_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\psi_{i}(x)\}>0
.
Suppose that
\bar{\theta}>0
, and

\mathop{\inf}\limits_{c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}}u_{i}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)=0
for some
i\in I
.
Then there exists
\{(t_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

s_{k}:=c_{*}t_{k}-x_{k}\cdot e\geq\bar{z},\quad\mathop{\lim}\limits_{k\to\infty}u_{i}^{\bar{\theta}}(t_{k},x_{k})=0.

Noting that
\mathop{\lim}\limits_{\varsigma\to\infty}\mathop{\inf}\limits_{c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\varsigma}u_{i}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)\geq\bar{\theta}\mathop{\min}\limits_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}\psi_{i}(x)>0
,
the sequence
\{s_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded from above.
Writing
x_{k}=x_{k}^{\prime}+x_{k}^{\prime\prime}
with
x_{k}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}
and
x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
,
and assume up to an extraction of a subsequence that

s_{k}\to s_{\infty}\geq\bar{z}
and
x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\to x_{\infty}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}

as
k\to\infty
.
Let

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{k}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\bm{u}^{\bar{\theta}}(t+t_{k},x+x_{k}^{\prime})=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{k}+x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\cdot e+\eta_{0})+\bar{\theta}\bm{\Psi}(x)-\bm{u}(t+t_{k},x+x_{k}^{\prime}).

Observing that
\bm{u}_{k}^{\bar{\theta}}
is uniformly bounded and nonnegative in

\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}-s_{k}-x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\cdot e\}
.
Up to an extraction of a subsequence,

\{\bm{u}(\cdot+t_{k},\cdot+x_{k}^{\prime})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to a solution
\bm{u}_{\infty}

of (
1.4
) and
\{\bm{u}^{\bar{\theta}}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to a function

\bm{u}_{\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}
,
uniformly in any compact subset of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{\infty}+x_{\infty}\cdot e+\eta_{0})+\bar{\theta}\bm{\Psi}(x)-\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x),

and
\bm{u}_{\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)\geq\bm{0}
for any

(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}-s_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e\}
.
In particular,

u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}(0,x_{\infty})=\mathop{\lim}\limits_{k\to\infty}u_{i}^{\bar{\theta}}(t_{k},x_{k})=0
.
In view of (
5.11
) and (
5.14
),

\bm{u}_{k}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)\geq\bar{\theta}\mathop{\min}\limits_{x}\bm{\Psi}(x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\bar{z}-s_{k}-x_{k}^{\prime\prime}\cdot e\},

and hence
\bm{u}_{\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)\gg\bm{0}
for any

(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq\bar{z}-s_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e\}

by passing the limits.
Therefore
s_{\infty}>\bar{z}
since
u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}(0,x_{\infty})=0
.
Furthermore, it is easy to see that

(1-\varrho^{*})\bm{1}\leq\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x)\leq\bm{1},\quad(1-\varrho^{*})\bm{1}\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+s_{\infty}+x_{\infty}\cdot e+\eta_{0})\leq\bm{1}

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}-s_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e\}
.
By a direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\quad\frac{\partial u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)}{\partial t}-d_{i}(x)\Delta u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}-q_{i}(x)\cdot\nabla u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}

\displaystyle=f_{i}(x,U)-f_{i}(x,\bm{u}_{\infty})+\bar{\theta}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})\psi_{k}-\mu^{-}\psi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\tau U+(1-\tau)\bm{u}_{\infty})d\tau}\right)(u_{k,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}-\bar{\theta}\psi_{k})}+\bar{\theta}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})\psi_{k}-\mu^{-}\psi_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{i}}(x,\tau U+(1-\tau)\bm{u}_{\infty})d\tau}\right)u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bar{\theta}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\left[\int_{0}^{1}\left(\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\bm{1})-\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{k}}(x,\tau U+(1-\tau)\bm{u}_{\infty})\right)d\tau\right]\psi_{k}}-\mu^{-}\psi_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\int_{0}^{1}{\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial u_{i}}(x,\tau U+(1-\tau)\bm{u}_{\infty})d\tau}\right)u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:c_{*}t-x\cdot e>\bar{z}-s_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e\}
.
The maximum principle then implies that

u_{i,\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}\equiv 0
for any

(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e\geq\bar{z}-s_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e\}\cap\{t\leq 0\}
,
which contradicts the fact that
\bm{u}_{\infty}^{\bar{\theta}}(t,x)>\bm{0}

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e=\bar{z}-s_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e\}
.
Therefore
\bar{\theta}=0
, and hence (
5.15
) holds.

To this end, we conclude from (
5.11
), (
5.14
) and (
5.15
) that

U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta_{0})\geq\bm{u}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Recall that
\eta_{0}\in[\frac{\bar{\eta}}{2},\bar{\eta})
, this contradicts the definition of
\bar{\eta}
. Therefore
\bar{\eta}=0
, and consequently,

U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)\geq\bm{u}(t,x),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Similarly, if we define

\underline{\eta}=\inf\left\{\eta\geq 0:\ \bm{u}(t,x)\geq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e-\eta),\ \forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\right\},

then
0\leq\bar{\eta}\leq-\underline{s}
.
One can prove by using analogous arguments as above that
\underline{\eta}=0
.
Therefore
\bm{u}(t,x)\geq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e)
for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
The proof is complete.
∎

Theorem 5.12
.

Assume (H1)-(H8).
Let
U(x,ct-x\cdot e)
be a pulsating traveling front of (
1.4
) with
c\geq c_{+}^{0}
,
and
\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})
be a solution of (
1.4
) with
initial value
\bm{u}(0,\cdot;\bm{u}_{0})=\bm{u}_{0}\in Y_{+}
.
Assume that
\bm{0}<\bm{u}_{0}<\bm{1}
satisfies (
5.2
) and (
5.3
).
Then there exists
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\lim_{t\to\infty}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}}|\bm{u}(t,x;\bm{u}_{0})-U(x,ct-x\cdot e+s_{0})|=0.

Proof.

We only prove the case
c=c_{*}
since the other one can be proved similarly.
Let
s_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
be such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\varsigma\to-\infty}\left\{\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\\
-x\cdot e\leq\varsigma\end{subarray}}\left|\frac{U(x,-x\cdot e+s_{0})}{k|x\cdot e|e^{-\lambda_{*}(x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)}-\bm{1}\right|\right\}=0.

Assume without loss of generality that
s_{0}=0
.
If the statement is not true, then there exist
\alpha>0
and a sequence

\{(t_{n},x_{n})\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

t_{n}\to+\infty\ (n\to\infty),\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}|u_{i}(t_{n},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})-U_{i}(x_{n},c_{*}t_{n}-x_{n}\cdot e)|\geq\alpha

(5.16)

for some
i\in I
. Denote
s_{n}=c_{*}t_{n}-x_{n}\cdot e
. If
\{s_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded,
we write
x_{n}=x_{n}^{\prime}+x_{n}^{\prime\prime}
with
x_{n}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}
and

x_{n}^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
.
Assume up to a subsequence that

s_{n}\to s_{\infty}
and
x_{n}^{\prime\prime}\to x_{\infty}\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}
as
n\to\infty
,
and set
t_{n}^{\prime}=t_{n}-t_{\infty}
, where
t_{\infty}:=\frac{s_{\infty}+x_{\infty}\cdot e}{c_{*}}
.
Let

\bm{u}_{n}(t,x)=\bm{u}(t+t_{n}^{\prime},x+x_{n}^{\prime};\bm{u}_{0}),

then
\bm{u}_{n}
is a solution of (
5.1
) in

(t,x)\in(-t_{n}^{\prime},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
for each
n
.
Up to a subsequence, we assume that
\{\bm{u}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to a solution
\bm{u}_{\infty}
of (
5.1
) uniformly in any compact subset of
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
.
In view of Lemma
5.9
,

\displaystyle U(x,s_{n}^{\prime}-\sigma_{*})-\delta_{*}|\bm{\xi}_{*}|e^{-\beta(t+t_{n}^{\prime})}\bm{1}\leq\bm{u}_{n}(t,x)\leq U(x,s_{n}^{\prime}+\sigma_{*})+\delta_{*}|\bm{\xi}_{*}|e^{-\beta(t+t_{n}^{\prime})}\bm{1}

for any
(t,x)\in[t_{*}-t_{n}^{\prime},+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{N}
, where
s_{n}^{\prime}:=c_{*}(t+t_{n}^{\prime})-(x+x_{n}^{\prime})\cdot e
.
By passing the limits and noting that
c_{*}t_{n}^{\prime}-x_{n}^{\prime}\cdot e\to 0
and
t_{n}^{\prime}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
,

\displaystyle U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e-\sigma_{*})

\displaystyle\leq\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x)\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\sigma_{*}),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

On the other hand, it follows from Lemma
5.10
that for any
\eta>0
, there exist
s_{\eta}\in\mathbb{R}
and
D_{\eta}>0
such that

\displaystyle\quad U(x,s_{n}^{\prime}-\eta)-D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}s_{n}^{\prime}}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}s_{n}^{\prime}}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{u}_{n}(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq U(x,s_{n}^{\prime}+\eta)+D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}s_{n}^{\prime}}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}s_{n}^{\prime}}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ t\geq-t_{n}^{\prime},\ s_{n}^{\prime}\leq s_{\eta}\}
.
By passing the limits,

\displaystyle\quad U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e-\eta)-D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e+\eta)+D_{\eta}e^{\lambda_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}}(x)-e^{\epsilon_{*}(c_{*}t-x\cdot e)}\bm{\Phi}_{\lambda_{*}+\epsilon_{*}}(x)\right)

for any
(t,x)\in\{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}:\ c_{*}t-x\cdot e\leq s_{\eta}\}
.
It then follows from Lemma
5.11
that

\bm{u}_{\infty}(t,x)=U(x,c_{*}t-x\cdot e),\quad\forall\;(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}.

In particular,

u_{i,\infty}(t_{\infty},x_{\infty})=U_{i}(x_{\infty},c_{*}t_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e)
.
Noting that

\displaystyle u_{i,\infty}(t_{\infty},x_{\infty})

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}u_{i}(t_{n},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0}),

\displaystyle U_{i}(x_{\infty},c_{*}t_{\infty}-x_{\infty}\cdot e)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}U_{i}(x_{n},c_{*}t_{n}-x_{n}\cdot e),

which contradicts (
5.16
). Now if
s_{n}\to-\infty
as
n\to\infty
,
up to an extraction of a subsequence, Lemma
5.9
yields that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\bm{u}(t_{n},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})=\lim_{n\to\infty}U(x_{n},c_{*}t_{n}-x_{n}\cdot e)=\bm{0}
,
and if
s_{n}\to+\infty
as
n\to\infty
, then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\bm{u}(t_{n},x_{n};\bm{u}_{0})=\lim_{n\to\infty}U(x_{n},c_{*}t_{n}-x_{n}\cdot e)=\bm{1}
,
both contradict (
5.16
). Hence the statement is true.
The proof is complete.
∎
